## BOOK II (Sects 47-64)

## Anacephalaeosis IV

These are the contents also of this first tome of the second book, which is the fourth in the numbered sequence of the tomes from the beginning, in which there are eighteen sects:

**Anaceph. IV 47** 47. Encratites, who are an offshoot of Tatian, rejecting marriage and saying that it is of Satan, and forbidding all eating of things that have life.

**Anaceph. IV 48** 48. Phrygians, also called Montanists and Tascodrugites, who accept the Old and the New Testament, but bring in other prophets after the prophets, boasting of a certain Montanus and Priscilla.

**Anaceph. IV 49,1** 49. Pepuzians, also called Quintillianists, with whom the Artotyrites are joined: they are of these Phrygians, but teach other things besides them, deifying a certain deserted town, Pepuza, between Galatia and Cappadocia and Phrygia, and indeed regarding it as Jerusalem (there is also another Pepuza), and granting to women the right to rule and to act as priests.

**Anaceph. IV 49,2a** And they perform their initiation by piercing a certain young child.

**Anaceph. IV 49,2b** And indeed they tell the fable that Christ was revealed there in Pepuza to Quintilla, or else to Priscilla, in the form of a woman.

**Anaceph. IV 49,3** And they likewise use the Old and the New Testament, altering them according to their own mind.

**Anaceph. IV 50** 50. Quartodecimans, who keep the Passover on one day of the year, on whatever day the fourteenth of the moon falls, whether on a sabbath or on a Lord’s day, both fasting and at the same time keeping vigil on that day.

**Anaceph. IV 51** 51. Alogi, so called by us, who reject the Gospel of John and the Word who is in it, God ever from above from the Father, not accepting the very Gospel according to John, nor his Revelation.

**Anaceph. IV 52,1** 52. Adamians, called by some Adamites, whose doctrine is one of mockery rather than of truth.

**Anaceph. IV 52,2a** For men and women gather together in one place naked, as they came from their mother.

**Anaceph. IV 52,2b** And so they perform their readings and prayers and everything whatever, being, if you please, solitaries and continent and not accepting marriage, and regarding their church as paradise.

**Anaceph. IV 53,1** 53. Sampsaeans, also called Elkesaeans, who to this day inhabit Arabia, the country lying above the Dead Sea; who, deceived by a certain false prophet, Elchasai,

**Anaceph. IV 53,2** of whose family Marthus and Marthana, two women worshipped by the sect as goddesses, were still living until lately, hold views in all things closely like those of the Ebionites.

**Anaceph. IV 54a** 54. Theodotians, who derive from Theodotus the cobbler, from Byzantium.

**Anaceph. IV 54b** He was eminent in Greek learning, but, being with others in the days of the persecution of that time, he alone fell away while they bore witness, and because he was reproached he devised the saying that Christ is a mere man, so that he might not come under the charge of having denied God; and so he taught.

**Anaceph. IV 55** 55. Melchizedekians, who honour Melchizedek, saying that he is a certain power and not a mere man, and who have dared to refer and ascribe everything to his name.

**Anaceph. IV 56a** 56. Bardesianists.

**Anaceph. IV 56b** This Bardesanes came from Mesopotamia; at first he was of the true faith and distinguished in wisdom, but, turning aside from the truth, he taught much as Valentinus did, apart from a few points [in] which he differs from Valentinus.

**Anaceph. IV 57,1a** 57. Noetians.

**Anaceph. IV 57,1b** This Noetus was from Smyrna in Asia.

**Anaceph. IV 57,1c** Carried away by conceit, he, with certain others, taught that Christ is the Son-Father, [saying] that the same is Father and Son and Holy Spirit.

**Anaceph. IV 57,2** And he said that he himself was Moses, and he said that his brother was Aaron.

**Anaceph. IV 58,1a** 58. Valesians.

**Anaceph. IV 58,1b** These, as we have understood, inhabit Bacathus, the chief village of Arabia of Philadelphia, and make eunuchs of those who chance upon them and lodge as guests with them; and most of them too are eunuchs, having been castrated.

**Anaceph. IV 58,2** And they teach certain other things full of heresy, doing away with [the things] of the law and the prophets, and bringing in certain other obscenities.

**Anaceph. IV 59** 59. Cathari, who joined with Novatus the Roman, and who reject the twice-married altogether and do not accept repentance.

**Anaceph. IV 60a** 60. Angelics.

**Anaceph. IV 60b** These have died out completely; and they boasted that they held an angelic rank, or else on account of having invoked angels.

**Anaceph. IV 61a** 61. Apostolics, also called Apotactics, they too [living] around Pisidia, accepting only Apotactics, and praying by themselves.

**Anaceph. IV 61b** They resemble the Encratites, but hold other views besides theirs.

**Anaceph. IV 62** 62. Sabellians, who hold views like those of the Noetians, except that they say the Father did not suffer.

**Anaceph. IV 63a** 63. Origenists, those of a certain Origen.

**Anaceph. IV 63b** And these are doers of shameful things, committing unspeakable acts and giving over their own bodies to corruption.

**Anaceph. IV 64a** 64. Other Origenists, those of Origen, also called Adamantius, the writer, who reject the resurrection of the dead and introduce Christ and the Holy Spirit as creatures, allegorising paradise and the heavens and all other things, and talking nonsense to the effect that the kingdom of Christ will come to an end.

**Anaceph. IV 64b** These again are the eighteen sects of the second book, and of its first tome.

### Sect 47: Encratites

Against the Encratites, the twenty-seventh, but the forty-seventh in the series.

**47,1,1a** Certain people called Encratites are the successors of this Tatian, by their very nature led astray and deceived by him.

**47,1,1b** Yet they have come to think other things beyond him, and have given themselves over once again to a greater frivolity.

**47,1,2a** These are numerous even to this day in Pisidia and in the Phrygia that is called Burnt.

**47,1,2b** For perhaps it is by God’s dispensation that the homeland too has taken the name it is called by in the world, because its inhabitants have been burnt by the perversion of an error of this kind and of this size; for there are many heresies in that region.

**47,1,3** They are found also in parts of Asia, and in the land of the Isaurians, Pamphylians and Cilicians, and in Galatia; and by now [in] part of the city of the Romans as well, and also in that of the Antiochenes of Syria, though not everywhere, [...] this heresy.

**47,1,4** These too say that there are certain first principles: the [power] of the devil, set against the works of God — the devil not being subject to God, but strong and acting as by his own authority, and not as having come to be in a state of turning aside; for they do not speak according to the church, but otherwise, contrary to the preaching of the truth.

**47,1,5** As their principal scriptures they use the writings called the Acts of Andrew, of John and of Thomas, together with some apocryphal works, and whichever words of the Old Testament they choose.

**47,1,6** Marriage they plainly declare to be of the devil; and they abhor things that have a soul, forbidding them not for the sake of self-control nor of a way of life, but out of fear and a fancy that they would be condemned for partaking of things that have a soul.

**47,1,7** They too make use of mysteries with water; but wine they do not partake of at all, declaring it a thing of the devil, and those who drink it and those who use it to be lawless and sinners.

**47,1,8** They also believe in a resurrection of the dead, so that among those thus led astray everything is full of madness.

**47,1,9** For indeed the man of understanding may see, and marvel, and be struck with amazement at anything whatever of all that these strangers say and do, since everything among them is said and done without coherence and admits no semblance of truth.

**47,2,1** For if they use the Old and the New Testament, how will differing first principles be found, when the two Testaments agree in proclaiming the knowledge of one [...] Godhead?

**47,2,2a** And if there is indeed to be a resurrection of the dead, how will venerable marriage still be found to be of the devil?

**47,2,2b** Seeing that God says, “Grow and multiply”, and in the gospel the Lord says, “What God has yoked together, let not man separate”, and the apostle declares, “Marriage is honourable and the bed undefiled”.

**47,2,3** But when they are refuted they blaspheme, calling him a drunkard; and to support their own opinion and to set up a myth against those who drink wine, they seize on certain passages, hunting them down like wild beasts, and say that this kind of thing is from the evil one: “For Noah,” it says, “drank of the wine and was stripped naked, and Lot,” it says,

**47,2,4a** “having become drunk, lay with his own daughters in ignorance; and through drunkenness came about the making of the calf; and the scripture says, ‘Who has tumult?

**47,2,4b** Who has fights?

**47,2,4c** Who has disgust and idle talk?

**47,2,4d** Who has bruises without cause?

**47,2,4e** Whose eyes are livid?

**47,2,4f** Is it not those who linger over wine, is it not those who track out where drinking-bouts are held?’”

**47,2,5** And tracking out certain other things of this sort, they heap them together to make themselves plausible, not knowing that everything without measure is grievous in every case, and stands forbidden as lying outside what is proper.

**47,2,6a** For indeed I would say this not of wine only, but of every kind of greed as well.

**47,2,6b** For the Lord too taught this, saying, “Let not your hearts,” he says, “be weighed down with carousing and drunkenness and the cares of this life”, and the saying, “If you are rather greedy, do not desire the rich man’s foods; for these belong to a false life”.

**47,2,7** And still more the holy apostle, cutting off the insatiable and the gluttonous, and pronouncing a curse on ravenous desires, said, “Foods for the belly, and the belly for foods; but God will bring to nothing both it and them”.

**47,2,8** Esau too lost his birthright through a boiled dish of wheat, according to the scripture, which calls one and the same dish a boiled dish of wheat and of lentils (but I think perhaps that it was not of wheat, that is, of grain, but perhaps that, as it was a leftover of lentils already boiled and put on the fire once more, the scripture described what had been fired a second time as a [boiled dish] of wheat, because it was heated up again after it had gone cold).

**47,2,9a** And as the one was stripped naked through wine and suffered no wrong, so this one rather, having lost his birthright through hunger and greed, has suffered wrong beyond what wine did.

**47,2,9b** And a counterfeited phrase has no strength in the presence of the truth, nor does some contrived, mythical piece of play-acting.

**47,3,1** They pride themselves, forsooth, on self-control, while doing everything perilously: they are found in the midst of women, deceiving women on every side, travelling about with women and living with them and being waited upon by such women, being outside the truth, “having acquired only the outward form, but having denied the power of godliness itself”.

**47,3,2a** For whatever part of a work one leaves out, one has given up the whole of its fullness through the one part that was left out.

**47,3,2b** Hence the mysteries among them are performed with water only, and are not mysteries at all, but false ones performed in imitation of the true.

**47,3,3** Hence in this too they will be refuted by the Saviour’s straightforward word, which says, “I shall not drink from this yield of this vine until I drink it new in the kingdom of the heavens with you”.

**47,3,4** Once more, then, having pushed this one aside by the strong hand of the truth, like some painful vermin whose teeth have been drawn, let us go on to those that follow, calling as usual upon the God of all as guide and as defender against terrors, as helper of understanding and as himself the giver of wisdom,

**47,3,5** so that we, learning the truth from him, may be able to uncover the [...] of the others, and from the word of the truth to prepare against them a healing antidote from many aromatic kinds — for those already stricken, for their cure; for those who come upon it, for their healing; for those who are going to learn what they did not know, for the forestalling of the passions; and for us, for salvation and for a reward from God, to be bestowed ungrudgingly.

### Sect 48: Phrygians (Montanists)

Against those called Phrygians, that is, Montanists, or also Tascodrugites, the twenty-eighth, but the forty-eighth in the series.

**48,1,1** From these another heresy springs up in its turn, called that of the Phrygians, which arose at the same time as these and succeeded them.

**48,1,2** For these arose about the nineteenth year of Antoninus Pius, who came after Hadrian; and Marcion too, and Tatian’s followers, and the Encratites who succeeded him, in the times of Hadrian and after Hadrian.

**48,1,3** For these who are called Phrygians likewise receive the whole of scripture, Old Testament and New, and in the same way they affirm a resurrection of the dead; but they pride themselves on having one Montanus for a prophet, with Priscilla and Maximilla for prophetesses, and it is by heeding these that they have been turned aside.

**48,1,4** Concerning the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit they think as the holy catholic church does; but they have split themselves off, “giving heed to spirits of error and teachings of demons”, saying, “We must,” it says, “receive the gifts as well”.

**48,1,5** And the holy church of God likewise receives the gifts — but those that are gifts in truth, and that have already been tested in the holy church of God through the Holy Spirit by the prophets and apostles and by the Lord himself,

**48,1,6a** as the apostle John says in his epistle, “Test the spirits, whether they are of God”, and again says, “You have heard that Antichrist is coming, and now many Antichrists have arisen.

**48,1,6b** From among us they went out, yet they did not belong to us; for had they belonged to us, they would have stayed with us; but this was so that they might be shown not to belong to us.

**48,1,6c** For this reason I write to you, little children”, and so on.

**48,1,7a** Truly, then, these are not of the saints themselves.

**48,1,7b** For they went out through their own contentiousness, giving heed both to spirits of error and to fables.

**48,2,1a** For see, they are refuted from their very premise, since they are unable to fulfil what they promise in their contentiousness.

**48,2,1b** For if one must receive gifts, and there must be gifts in the church, how is it that after Montanus and Priscilla and Maximilla they no longer have prophets?

**48,2,1c** Has grace, then, ceased to work?

**48,2,1d** But grace does not cease to work in the holy church; God forbid!

**48,2,2** But if those who prophesied prophesied up to a certain time and [...] no longer prophesy, then neither Priscilla nor Maximilla prophesied, [...] after the prophecies that were tested through the holy apostles in the holy church.

**48,2,3** In two ways, then, their folly will be exposed: either they must demonstrate that there have been prophets since Maximilla, so that the grace of which they speak may not have ceased to work, or Maximilla and her companions will be found to be false prophets, who after the limit set for the prophetic gifts dared to be possessed, not by the Holy Spirit but by the error of demons, and to fill their hearers with delusions.

**48,2,4a** And see how the refutation against them will be spoken from the very things that are said among them.

**48,2,4b** For Maximilla, who is called a prophetess among them, says, “After me,” she says, “there will no longer be a prophet, but the consummation”.

**48,2,5** But see, on every side the Holy Spirit stands revealed, and so do the spirits of error; for whatever the prophets have said, and uttered with understanding and in full awareness, the things spoken by them have both been accomplished and are still being fulfilled.

**48,2,6** But she said that after her would come the consummation, and it has not yet been consummated, especially when so many emperors have come and so long a time has gone by.

**48,2,7** For from that time down to our own day — the twelfth year of Valentinian’s and Valens’ reign, and the [eighth] of Gratian’s — there are more or less two hundred and ninety years, and still the consummation foretold by her who boasted herself a prophetess has not come; she did not know even the day of her own death.

**48,2,8** And one can see how all who have made themselves strangers to the truth have not stayed in any steadfastness of reason, but, like infants entangled by the serpent that is ever deceiving, have given themselves over to destruction and to becoming the wolf’s food, being found outside the fold, to be dragged away and so to perish; not holding fast to the beginning, but forsaking the truth, they have handed themselves over to shipwreck and to the surge of every error.

**48,2,9a** For if Maximilla says that there will no longer be a prophet, then she does away with the gift’s being among them and being carried on to this day.

**48,2,9b** But if the gift remains until her time, then, as I said before, she herself had no share in the gifts either.

**48,3,1a** For she has gone astray; for the Lord sealed the church and fulfilled the gifts [in] it.

**48,3,1b** For when there was need of prophets, these same saints prophesied everything in a true spirit, with sound understanding and a mind in full awareness, being filled with the Holy Spirit, according to the proportion [of faith] of the gifts of the Spirit given to each, and according to the proportion of faith, for what is profitable.

**48,3,2a** What profitable thing, then, have these people said, or what in proportion to faith?

**48,3,2b** And how are these not rather the ones of whom the Lord said, “Be on your guard against the false prophets, who come to you in the garments of sheep, but within are rapacious wolves”?

**48,3,3** For comparing the things said by them with [those] in the Old and New Testament, which are true and came to pass in truth and were prophesied, let us test which is [really] prophecy and which is false prophecy.

**48,3,4** The prophet spoke everything with settled reasoning and full awareness, and uttered it from the Holy Spirit, saying everything with soundness, as did Moses, God’s attendant and faithful in the house; the prophet was called the seer [...] in the Old Testament.

**48,3,5a** “[For] the vision,” it says, “that the prophet Isaiah, son of Amoz, saw”; “I saw [the] Lord,” it says, “seated upon a lofty and exalted throne; and I beheld Seraphim and Cherubim, and heard the Lord say to me, ‘Go, say to this people: You shall hear with hearing and shall not understand, and seeing you shall see and shall not perceive’”.

**48,3,5b** And having heard from the Lord, he came to the people and said, “Thus says the Lord”.

**48,3,6** Do you not see that the word was that of a man in full awareness and not out of his mind, and that the utterance was not given forth as from an understanding beside itself?

**48,3,7** Likewise Ezekiel the prophet too, hearing from the Lord, “Make yourself bread on human dung”, said, “By no means, Lord; never has anything common or unclean entered my mouth”.

**48,3,8a** For knowing that the saying addressed to him by the Lord was spoken by way of threat, he did not set about doing this as one carried away in an ecstasy of the mind, but, keeping his understanding sound and in full awareness, he made his plea and said, “By no means, Lord”.

**48,3,8b** For such are both the teaching and the discourse of those who are truly prophets, whose understanding is sound in the Holy Spirit.

**48,3,9** And how is Daniel not found full of all understanding and in full awareness of his thoughts — he who solved the riddles for Nebuchadnezzar; and the things which that man beheld in dreams, and which had gone from the very one who had seen them,

**48,3,10** this man recalled to his memory, and at once supplied their interpretation, with a firm composure and an abundance of the gift, possessing understanding more abundantly than any man through the gift of the Holy Spirit, which truly makes wise the prophet and those who through the prophet are counted worthy of the teaching of the truth?

**48,3,11a** But in what these people profess to prophesy, they will be seen neither to be steady nor to have any coherence of speech.

**48,3,11b** For their sayings are crooked and uneven and hold to no straightness at all.

**48,4,1a** For straightway Montanus says, “Behold, the man is like a lyre, and I fly over him like a plectrum; the man sleeps, and I am awake.

**48,4,1b** Behold, it is the Lord who puts the hearts of men out of themselves and gives men a heart”.

**48,4,2** Who, then, among those who are in full awareness, who receive with understanding the word that profits and take care for their own life, will not condemn such a counterfeit premise, and the talk of one who boasts that he is numbered among the prophets, yet cannot say things like the prophets?

**48,4,3a** For neither did the Holy Spirit speak in him.

**48,4,3b** For to say, “I fly over and I strike and I am awake, and the Lord puts hearts out of themselves” — these are the words of a man in ecstasy and not of one in full awareness, but of one who displays another character beside that of the Holy Spirit, the Spirit which has spoken in the prophets.

**48,4,4** But if they will wish to weave falsehood into the truth and to befool the mind of those who care for exactness, they heap up arguments for themselves by which they dress up their own error, saying that certain things are alike, so as to establish, forsooth, [...] from the holy scripture’s having said, “God cast an ecstasy upon Adam and he slept”; yet this could no longer be like that.

**48,4,5** For God was not here too about to fashion a body from the one whom he was bringing into ecstasy, so as to bring on something similar through the abundance of his love for mankind.

**48,4,6a** For upon Adam he brought the ecstasy of sleep, not an ecstasy of the wits.

**48,4,6b** But ecstasy occurs in many different modes.

**48,4,6c** Ecstasy is said of an excess of wonder, and madness too is called ecstasy, because of standing out from what is proper.

**48,4,7** But that ecstasy of sleep was spoken of in another manner, according to the natural working, especially because it was brought most deeply upon the holy Adam, who had been fashioned by the hand of God.

**48,5,1a** And indeed one may truly see how rightly the divine scripture has called this an ecstasy.

**48,5,1b** For when a man sleeps, all the senses are moved out of their place, turned to rest, so that, so to say, the power of sight, though present, does not see; for the eye is shut, and the spirit that moves in the man, that is to say [the] soul, is at rest.

**48,5,2** When there is a bad smell in a house, or a sweet one, though the sense of smell is present, it does not take hold of it; for this sense has stood out of itself, turned to rest.

**48,5,3** When there are bitter or salty or sweet flavours in the mouth, the taste does not perceive them, for since it lies in the ecstasy of rest it is not at work, though in the man who is awake it was at work.

**48,5,4a** Hearing is present, but the faculty of hearing is idle as regards perception.

**48,5,4b** And often, when some people are talking in the house, unless the man is somewhat roused from sleep, he does not catch what is said by them, because the working of the faculty has withdrawn for the time being.

**48,5,5** When certain small creatures crawl over our body, we do not perceive their touch upon our body, unless the creatures set upon us heavily, since the whole instrument, through the rest of sleep, has stood out from its activity.

**48,5,6** For the instrument, being of earthly nature and set about the soul, because it was made thus by God to be useful to us, is granted a season which moves it from the clearest sensation into a state of rest; but the soul itself has not stood out of its ruling faculty, nor of its understanding.

**48,5,7** For often it pictures and sees itself as though awake, and walks about and works and sails the sea and addresses the people, and beholds itself through dreams in more and greater things than these;

**48,5,8** yet not in the manner of the senseless man who has come to be in ecstasy, a man beside himself, who while awake in body and soul busies himself with dreadful things and often deals dreadfully with himself and with his neighbours; for he does not know what he utters and does, since such a man has come to be in an ecstasy of folly.

**48,6,1** All this, beloved, we have been compelled to gather [concerning] the differences in the manners of ecstasy because of the saying, “The Lord cast an ecstasy upon Adam and he slept”.

**48,6,2** And we have explained for what reason the partaking of sleep is there called an ecstasy by the Lord: that it is because of God’s tender sparing and love for mankind, which the same God has bestowed on all men, so that a man may be carried over from anxiety, and from the needful business of life, into the rest of sleep;

**48,6,3** but there he has called it an ecstasy all the more because it made him feel no pain for the time, since he was going to take a rib from him and fashion it into a woman for him.

**48,6,4a** But he was not in an ecstasy of wits and thoughts.

**48,6,4b** For as soon as he arose he recognized her and said, “This now is bone out of my bones, and flesh out of my flesh; this one will be called woman, because she was taken out of her man”.

**48,6,5a** For he knows, as you see, both the first things and the present things, and he prophesies about the things to come.

**48,6,5b** For see, he recognized the first things, from when he was asleep, saying, “Bone out of my bones”; and he recognized the present things, perceiving, once the woman had been fashioned, that she was one removed from [his] body;

**48,6,6a** and he prophesied about what was to be: “For this reason a man will forsake his father and his mother and be joined to his wife, and the two will be one flesh”.

**48,6,6b** Such words belong not to a man in ecstasy, nor to one without awareness, but to one whose understanding is sound.

**48,7,1** And if something must also be said about the verse, “I said in my ecstasy, Every man is a liar”, its force in turn is different — not in the manner [...] of some senseless man beside himself (God forbid),

**48,7,2a** but of one marvelling exceedingly and reflecting, beyond the reasoning that is customary, [on] the things seen and done [im]measurably; for it is because the prophet marvelled that he speaks here too, through wonder.

**48,7,2b** The prophets came to be in ecstasy, but not in an ecstasy of their reasonings.

**48,7,3** For Peter too came to be in ecstasy, not as one failing to follow the word with awareness, but seeing, instead of the everyday course of things, other things besides those that are seen by men;

**48,7,4** “for he beheld a linen sheet being lowered, tied by its four corners, and in it all the four-footed creatures and creeping things and the birds of the sky”.

**48,7,5a** But see that the holy Peter was in full awareness and was not in an ecstasy of the wits.

**48,7,5b** For when he heard, “Rise, slay and eat”, he did not comply as one not sound in mind, but says to the Lord, “By no means, Lord; for never has anything common or unclean entered my mouth”.

**48,7,6a** And the holy David too said, “[I said:] Every man is a liar”.

**48,7,6b** Now he who says, “I said,” was speaking for himself, and said of men that they lie;

**48,7,7** so he himself was not lying, but, struck with amazement and marvelling at God’s love for mankind and at what had been proclaimed to him by the Lord, he marvelled beyond measure; and seeing every man in need of God’s mercy, he ascribed truthfulness to the Lord alone, and knew every man to be under penalty, so as to display the true Spirit, the one that spoke in the prophets and disclosed to them the deep things of the precise knowledge of God.

**48,7,8a** And Abraham too came to be in ecstasy, not in an ecstasy of the wits but in an ecstasy of fear.

**48,7,8b** For he saw an oven and torches about the setting of the sun [...] and other prophets, seeing visions, spoke with sound understanding, as Moses says, “I am terrified and trembling”.

**48,7,9** And Abraham knew the things spoken by the Lord: “For knowing,” [it says], “you shall know that your seed shall be a sojourner in a foreign land four hundred years”.

**48,7,10** And you see how it may be perceived that everything was spoken by the prophets in truth, with sound understanding and sober reasoning, and not in derangement.

**48,8,1a** And if again they will wish to say [that] “the first gifts are not like the last”, from where can they show this?

**48,8,1b** For the holy prophets and holy apostles prophesied in the same manner as one another.

**48,8,2a** First, then, when the Saviour was ascending into heaven, those who saw the two in white garments did not see them in derangement, but heard with sound understanding, “Men of Galilee, why do you stand gazing into heaven?

**48,8,2b** This Jesus, who was taken up from you into heaven, will come in the same way”, and so on.

**48,8,3** Then Peter too, as I said, seeing and hearing and answering and saying, “By no means, Lord”, was steady in his understanding.

**48,8,4** For Agabus, when prophesying, spoke, and signifying it in a riddle with a marvellous gesture, took Paul’s belt, bound his own feet, and says, “The man whose belt this is, him they will bind and carry off to Jerusalem”.

**48,8,5a** And again, too, prophets came down to Antioch and announced that there would be a famine over the whole inhabited world.

**48,8,5b** And their word did not fall to the ground; but so as to prove them true prophets, the scripture at once adds, saying, “which took place under Claudius Caesar”.

**48,8,6** And Paul, the most holy apostle, when prophesying, said, “But the Spirit says expressly, in the last days hard times will set in”, and so on,

**48,8,7** and again elsewhere, “Some will depart from the sound teaching, giving heed to deceiving [spirits] and teachings of demons, forbidding to marry, to abstain from foods, which God created for partaking by us who [receive them] with thanksgiving”.

**48,8,8** How plainly [this] has been fulfilled in you and in those like you is [clear] from the very matters before us; for most of these heresies forbid marrying and command abstaining from foods, not urging it for the sake of a way of life, nor for the sake of greater virtue and of prizes and crowns, but regarding these things made by the Lord as abominable.

**48,9,1** But the holy church both glorifies virginity and praises the single life and chastity and widowhood, and honours and accepts venerable marriage, while it forbids fornication and adultery and licentiousness,

**48,9,2** whence one may observe the distinctive stamp of the holy catholic church, and the counterfeit ways of the others, [so] that whoever has tested them flees from every error and from the crooked road and the path that lies uphill.

**48,9,3** For I said above how it was foretold by the most holy apostle, and I will say it again, that, making us secure and distinguishing the character of the holy church from the error of the heresies, he said that they boldly lay down laws forbidding what has been well ordained by God, when he spoke of those who forbid marrying, and abstaining from foods.

**48,9,4** For with a certain measure [...] God the Word, having said in the gospel, “Do you wish to become perfect?”, bearing with the fashioning and weakness of men, rejoices over those who are able to show forth the proofs of godliness and who choose to practise virginity and chastity and self-control, yet honours single marriage;

**48,9,5** even though he prefigured the gifts of the priesthood to the world chiefly through those who live in continence after a single marriage and those who continue in virginity, as his apostles too ordained the church’s rule of the priesthood in good order and holiness.

**48,9,6** But if anyone through weakness should need, after the death of his own wife, to be joined in a second marriage, the rule of the truth does not forbid this — that is, for one who is not a priest.

**48,9,7** But these people forbid it, according to the saying, “forbidding to marry”; for they cast out the man who has been joined in a second marriage, and compel people not to be joined in a second marriage.

**48,9,8** We, however, impose no compulsion, but we exhort with good counsel, urging on the one who is able; yet we impose no compulsion on the one who is not able, nor do we cast him out of life.

**48,9,9** For the holy word has proclaimed in every way that we should bear the weakness of the weak, as the holy apostle too will at once be found saying, and rebuking such people [...] as are not of the same gift, “Refuse younger widows;

**48,9,10a** for when they have grown wanton against Christ they wish to marry, incurring judgement, because they have set aside their first faith”.

**48,9,10b** For those who made a promise and set it aside incur judgement; but those who made no promise, and were joined in marriage through weakness, will not incur judgement.

**48,9,10c** But if they were going to incur judgement, how could he say, “Let them marry, let them rule their households”?

**48,10,1** Everyone who prophesies, then, is found to be in full awareness, be it in the Old Testament or be it in the New, as the holy John too said in the Revelation, “These things the Lord revealed to his own servants by means of his servant John”, and “Thus says the Lord”.

**48,10,2** And he who says these things had a sound understanding and was in full awareness; see, then, [...] “Thus says the Lord” and “A vision that he saw”.

**48,10,3a** But this Montanus, who boasts himself to be a prophet and has led astray those who have been led astray by him, recounts incoherent things contrary to the divine scripture.

**48,10,3b** For he says in his so-called prophecy, “Why do you speak of the one who is saved as above man?

**48,10,3c** For the righteous,” he says, “will shine a hundredfold beyond the sun, and the little ones among you who are saved will shine a hundredfold beyond the moon”.

**48,10,4** But the Lord refutes such a one — he who has the authority to grant that the faces of the saints should shine, who made the face of Moses radiant, and who transforms his saints at the coming resurrection of the bodies, those sown in dishonour and raised in glory, not other bodies besides those that exist,

**48,10,5** but the very ones that exist, raised whole, yet receiving glory from him after [the] resurrection, from him who ungrudgingly bestows the glory on his own saints, since he has the authority to give and to grant as God and Lord.

**48,10,6a** But though he has [the authority] to grant it, he made no promise such as Montanus makes, but says, “Your faces will shine like the sun”.

**48,10,6b** If, then, he who holds the authority and is truly our Master and Lord, Jesus Christ, declares that the faces of the righteous are to shine like the sun, how does Montanus promise more, a hundredfold?

**48,10,7** Unless indeed he is like that one who promised Adam and those with him, “You shall be as gods”, and who brought it about that they were cast out of the glory that was theirs and of the delight of paradise, and were brought down into the corruption of death.

**48,11,1** Further, the same Montanus adds, speaking thus: “I, the Lord God, the Almighty, am residing in a man”.

**48,11,2** So then, the divine scripture keeps us well safeguarded, and so does the course of the Holy Spirit’s teaching, admonishing us, so that we may know what the imitations of the alien spirit are, and the things opposed to the truth.

**48,11,3a** For when Montanus said this straight out, he prompted us to call to mind the things spoken by the Lord.

**48,11,3b** For thus says the Lord in the gospel, “I have come in my Father’s name, and you did not receive me.

**48,11,4a** Another will come under his own name, and him they will receive”.

**48,11,4b** Such a one, then, has been found wholly out of harmony with the divine scriptures, as would be plain to anyone who reads them with attention.

**48,11,4c** If, then, he is out of harmony, he [himself] is a stranger to the holy catholic church, and so is the heresy that follows him, boasting that it has prophets and gifts, which it has not received, but from which it has departed.

**48,11,5a** Who, then, being in full awareness, would dare to call these people prophets, and would not rather [say] that such people are deceivers?

**48,11,5b** For Christ taught us, saying, “I send you the Spirit, the Paraclete”, and giving the signs he said, “He will glorify me”;

**48,11,6a** so that one may see in truth that the holy apostles, having received the Spirit, the Paraclete, glorified the Lord, whereas this Montanus glorifies himself;

**48,11,6b** the Lord glorified his Father, and the Lord Christ in turn glorified the Spirit, calling it the Spirit of truth; but Montanus glorifies himself alone, and declares himself to be the Almighty Father, and that which came to dwell in him [...], so that it might be shown from every side that he is not that one, nor sent by him, nor receiving from him.

**48,11,7** “For in the Lord all the fullness of the Godhead was pleased to dwell bodily”, and, “From his fullness all the prophets have received”, according to the word of the holy John.

**48,11,8** And see how all the ancients proclaimed Christ, and those who came afterwards glorified Christ and confessed him; but Montanus has thrust himself in, calling himself somebody, so that it might be shown that he is not that one, nor has been sent by him, nor has received anything from him.

**48,11,9a** Then again the pitiable little man Montanus says, “Not an angel, not an envoy; it is I, the Lord God, the Father, who have come”.

**48,11,9b** But in saying this he will be caught out as an alien, not glorifying Christ, whom every gift of the church, given in truth in the holy church, has glorified.

**48,11,10a** For he will be found to be outside the body of the church and of the head of all things, and “not holding fast the head, from which the whole body, fitted together, grows”, as it is written.

**48,11,10b** For our Lord Jesus Christ showed himself to be the Son, being really the Son in truth, but Montanus calls himself the Father as well.

**48,12,1** How then, O Phrygians, who have risen up against us once again as enemies, who have put on the form of the Christian name, yet have taken up the warfare of barbarians and imitate the hostility of the Trojans and Phrygians — how are we to believe you when you say that you have departed from the church because of gifts?

**48,12,2** For things that are foreign to gifts, and contrary to the character promised by the Lord, such as your prophets utter, could no longer be gifts.

**48,12,3** And you bring Maximilla before us once more; for indeed even your names are outlandish and most fearsome, with nothing gentle or very sweet about them, but something wild and barbarous.

**48,12,4** For straightway this Maximilla, among those who are so called after the Phrygians — hear, O children of Christ, what she says: “Do not listen to me, but listen to Christ”.

**48,12,5a** And in the very words in which she seemed to glorify Christ she has gone astray.

**48,12,5b** For if she were of Christ, she would have spoken in the manner of the holy apostles, as each [of them] speaks: Peter first, saying, “We heard from him”, and the Lord himself saying, “He who hears you hears me”, and Paul saying, “Become my imitators, even as I am Christ’s”.

**48,12,6a** But she tells the truth while lying, and against her will.

**48,12,6b** For she speaks rightly in saying that they should give ear not to her but to Christ.

**48,12,6c** For often the unclean spirits are compelled to bring themselves down, [as] not being of the truth, and to give a glimpse of their Lord, willing and unwilling, through compulsion;

**48,12,7a** as the slave-girl who had a spirit of Python said, “These men are servants of God the Most High”, and the saying, “Why have you come before the time to torment us?

**48,12,7b** We know you, who you are, the Holy One of God”; so too this woman, under compulsion, said that people should heed, not her, but Christ.

**48,12,8a** How, then, can those who heard this from her lips and put their faith in her want to give her a hearing, when it was from her that they learned to heed not her but the Lord?

**48,12,8b** For truly, since she was uttering earthly things, if they had had any understanding, she ought not to have been listened to.

**48,12,9a** And do not say that she was in full awareness.

**48,12,9b** For it is not the mark of one in full awareness to teach and to overturn oneself.

**48,12,9c** For if she says at all, “Do not listen to me”, what kind of spirit was it that spoke in her?

**48,12,10a** For if it was she herself, or a human being, who was speaking, it follows that she was not in the Holy Spirit.

**48,12,10b** For she who says, “Do not listen to me”, was plainly speaking what is human, and was not in the Holy Spirit.

**48,12,10c** But if, not being in the Holy Spirit from above, but being of human mind, she knew nothing, then she was no prophetess, not having the Holy Spirit, but spoke and uttered with a human mind.

**48,12,11a** But if she spoke and prophesied by the Holy Spirit, what kind of Holy Spirit, then, was it that says, “Do not listen to me”?

**48,12,11b** Great indeed is the blindness of the deceit, and great is God’s word, which in every way gives us understanding to know which things have been spoken by the Holy Spirit, sometimes in the person of the Father, sometimes in the person of the Son, and sometimes in the person of the Holy Spirit.

**48,12,12** And if the spirit in Maximilla had been the Holy [Spirit], it would not have forbidden its own words; “for the Holy Spirit is one, which distributes to each as it wills”.

**48,12,13** But if it has authority to distribute as it wills, and bears the name Spirit of knowledge and Spirit of godliness, and is called the Spirit of Christ and the Spirit of the Father — the Spirit that goes forth from the Father and takes of the Son — not being alien to Father and Son, then it would not say, “Do not listen to me”.

**48,12,14** For the Spirit spoke the things of Christ, and Christ sends the Spirit and by the Holy Spirit casts out the demons, and the Son speaks the things of the Father, and the Father consecrated the Son and dispatched him into the world, so that they may know him and glorify him, even as they glorify the Father.

**48,12,15** And in every way the thinking of those who estrange themselves from the following of Christ has fallen to the ground.

**48,13,1** And again the same Maximilla — the knowledge and teaching of full awareness, if I may speak mockingly as well — says, “The Lord has sent me as adherent, revealer and interpreter of this labour and covenant and promise, compelled, willing and unwilling, to make known the knowledge of God”.

**48,13,2** Let us consider, then, beloved, the steady footing of our life and the way of light, and let us not stumble through the words of the adversary and of the creature that the alien spirit feeds.

**48,13,3a** For see here the one who spoke thus, declaring himself compelled, not acting by free choice.

**48,13,3b** But it was not against his will that our Lord came into the world, nor was he sent by the Father under compulsion; rather, together with the Father he has the willing, and together with the Holy Spirit the bestowing;

**48,13,4** and as he himself, together with the Father, has the willing, and the acting not under compulsion, and the giving of grace to all, but through the abundance of his love for mankind, so too those whom he called he has called with their free choice, not laying compulsion on them, not putting a collar on them.

**48,13,5** For he says, “You who thirst, come to me”, and again, “If anyone wishes to come to me, let him deny himself and follow me”, and through Isaiah, uttering the same thing, he said, “If you are willing and hearken to me”; and afterwards, so that the prophet might show who it is that speaks, he said, “For the mouth of the Lord has spoken these things”.

**48,13,6** And do you see in every way the discord of these people with the divine writing, and their notion and assumption, so different from the faith and the order of God?

**48,13,7a** For Maximilla too said that she compels the willing and the unwilling, so that from the very utterance such a woman will be found to lie.

**48,13,7b** For she neither instructed the willing in a knowledge of God of which she herself was ignorant, nor compelled the unwilling.

**48,13,8a** At any rate, not all who are in the world know what the name of Maximilla is, nor the false utterance of her words.

**48,13,8b** And in every way the deluded notion of these people has fallen to the ground, since it is not of the truth of God.

**48,14,1** These people also honour a certain deserted place in Phrygia, a city once called Pepuza but now razed to the ground, and they say that the Jerusalem from above comes down there.

**48,14,2a** Hence, going off there, they perform certain mysteries in the place and sanctify [themselves], as they suppose.

**48,14,2b** For this kind is found also in Cappadocia and Galatia, and in the aforesaid Phrygia, from which the heresy is called that of the Phrygians; but also in Cilicia, and mostly in Constantinople.

**48,14,3** And so that we may leave out nothing concerning the name of each heresy that has been set out by us, we shall speak again about the name of the Tascodrugites as well; for this name belongs either to this very heresy or to the one after it, called that of the Quintillianists; for this name too takes its start from these very people.

**48,14,4** And they are called Tascodrugites for this reason: among them a peg is called taskos, and drouggos is what the nostril, or snout, is called; and from their putting the finger called the forefinger on the nostril when they pray, forsooth for the sake of a downcast look and self-chosen righteousness, they have been called by some Tascodrugites, that is, Peg-snouters.

**48,14,5** And in this heresy, or in its yoke-fellow, the one called that of the Quintillianists, or Priscillianists and Pepuzians, they say that a dreadful and unlawful deed is done.

**48,14,6** For at some festival they stab a little child, quite an infant, over the entire body with pins of bronze, and so procure its blood for themselves, supposedly for the performance of a sacrifice.

**48,15,1a** What we have said about this heresy too is enough for us, beloved.

**48,15,1b** For we promised to grudge nothing about each heresy that we know, but to bring to light what we have grasped, whether by hearsay, or from treatises, or from documents, or from certain people who have truly confirmed our understanding,

**48,15,2** so that, making nothing more of anything than what we have come to know, we may [not] seem to stand guilty in conscience, as though we too were inventing things that are not right to say against people, and falling into the very failing into which they too have fallen, not speaking the truth, but proclaiming what they have neither seen nor heard nor taken over from the Holy Spirit’s true teaching;

**48,15,3** and going through everything precisely, as I said, concerning each heresy, uncovering its terrors for the correction of those who come upon them, and by the overthrow drawn from divine words and from right reasonings making, so to speak, a healing remedy, preparing [it] in the Lord in two ways,

**48,15,4** for those who are passing through the disease, that they may recover from it and from so great a pain, and for those who have never yet fallen into it, [for] a preventive aid, so to speak,

**48,15,5** imparting the remedy of the truth to those of understanding, we too may be called disciples of the Lord’s disciples and of the Saviour himself, who is the helper of bodies and souls.

**48,15,6** And since we consider that our labour here concerning this heresy too is sufficient, let us give ourselves, with the power of Christ, to going on to those that follow, having crushed its venom and the hook-shaped poisons of its teeth on the wood of the truth of the cross, since it is like the haemorrhous viper, whose bane draws off the blood of the whole body of those it has bitten and so brings on death.

**48,15,7a** For this heresy too, and that of the Quintillianists, performs this.

**48,15,7b** For it pierces the body of an undefiled child and carries off the blood for partaking, [...] forsooth for initiation into the name of Christ, having led the deceived astray.

**48,15,8** But let us, going on to those that follow in the power of Christ, call upon his truth, so that, having tracked out the force of each error, and having caught it and refuted it, we may, as usual, give thanks to God in all things.

### Sect 49: Quintillianists (Pepuzians)

Against the Quintillianists, or Pepuzians, who are also Priscillianists, to whom the Artotyrites are joined, the twenty-ninth, but the forty-ninth in the series.

**49,1,1** The Quintillianists, again, who are also called Pepuzians, and are named Artotyrites and Priscillianists, are the same as [those] named after the Phrygians and take their start from them, but are divided from them in a certain manner.

**49,1,2** For these Quintillianists, or Priscillianists, say that in Pepuza either Quintilla or Priscilla ([for] I cannot say precisely), but one of them, as I said before, had fallen asleep in Pepuza, and that Christ came to her and slept together with her

**49,1,3** in this manner, as that deluded woman said: “In the form of a woman,” she says, “Christ came to me, arrayed in a bright robe, and put wisdom into me, and disclosed to me that this very place is holy and that it is here that Jerusalem comes down from heaven”.

**49,1,4** Therefore they say that even to this day certain women are thus initiated there in the place, and men too, so that, waiting there, the women or the men may behold Christ.

**49,1,5a** Women, at any rate, are called prophetesses among them.

**49,1,5b** But I do not know very clearly whether this is among them or among those named after the Phrygians; for they are together and hold the same opinion.

**49,2,1a** These use the Old and the New Testament, and likewise speak of a resurrection of the dead.

**49,2,1b** And they have Quintilla as their founder, together with Priscilla, the one who is also among those named after the Phrygians.

**49,2,2** And they bring forward many vain testimonies, giving thanks to Eve for having been the first to eat of the tree of understanding; and they call the sister of Moses a prophetess, as testimony for the women appointed to the clergy among them; and besides, it says, there were four daughters of Philip who prophesied.

**49,2,3** And often in their church some seven virgins come in carrying lamps, coming in white robes, forsooth, in order to prophesy to the people.

**49,2,4** And they, displaying a kind of inspired frenzy, work deceit on the people present and make them all weep, as though leading them to the sorrow of repentance, shedding tears and, with a certain show, lamenting the life of men.

**49,2,5** And among them women are bishops and women are presbyters, and so on; between whom, they say, there is no difference, “for in Christ Jesus there is no male and no female”.

**49,2,6a** These are the things we have learned.

**49,2,6b** And they call them Artotyrites from their setting out bread and cheese in their mysteries and so performing their mysteries.

**49,3,1a** All the mockery that befalls men [lies] in their departing from the right faith and turning to helplessness and to the varieties of frenzies and orgies.

**49,3,1b** For minds roused to Bacchic frenzy always carry away, for any cause whatever, those who do not hold the anchor of the truth, but give themselves over to [...].

**49,3,2** For even if among them women are appointed to the episcopate and the presbyterate because of Eve, let them hear the Lord saying, “Your turning shall be to your husband, and he shall rule over you”.

**49,3,3a** And the apostolic word too has escaped them, that “I do not permit a woman to speak, nor to domineer over a man”, and again, “For man is not from woman, but woman from man”, and, “Adam has not been deceived, but Eve, the first to be deceived, has come to be in transgression”.

**49,3,3b** And O, the prolific error of this world!

**49,3,4** Therefore, passing by this heresy as something toothless and full of folly, like a gecko [...], I shall go on to those that follow, beloved, calling upon God for the help of our lowliness and for the fulfilment of our promise.

### Sect 50: Quartodecimans

Against the Quartodecimans, the thirtieth, but the fiftieth in the series.

**50,1,1** From these two heresies, which were mingled together with one another, that of the Phrygians and that of the Quintillianists or Priscillianists, another heresy sprang up again in the world, called that of the Quartodecimans.

**50,1,2a** And these hold everything as the church does, but they fall short in everything because they do not attend to the order and the teaching of the ordinance, attending still to Jewish myths.

**50,1,2b** And they do not even teach consistently with themselves: “for they know neither what they say nor the things about which they make assertions”.

**50,1,3** For once in the year, on a single day, these people celebrate the Passover in a contentious spirit, though they hold well and equally [with us] concerning the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, receiving the prophets and apostles and evangelists, and likewise confessing the resurrection of the flesh, a judgement to come, and life eternal.

**50,1,4** But these have fallen into a mistake, and no ordinary one, forsooth relying on the text in which the law said, “Accursed is whoever does not observe the Passover on the month’s fourteenth day”.

**50,1,5** And others of them, keeping the same one day and fasting on the same one day and celebrating the mysteries, boast that they have found the exact date from the Acts of Pilate, forsooth, in which it is recorded that the Saviour suffered on the eighth day before the Kalends of April.

**50,1,6** And they wish to keep the Passover on that day, on whichever day the fourteenth of the moon may fall; but those in Cappadocia keep the same one day, the eighth before the Kalends of April.

**50,1,7** And among them too there is no small dissension, some saying the fourteenth of the moon, and others the eighth before the Kalends of April.

**50,1,8** Moreover, we have come upon copies of the Acts of Pilate indicating that the passion took place on the fifteenth day before the Kalends of April; in truth, however, as we have learned with great precision, we have ascertained that the Saviour suffered on the thirteenth day before the Kalends of April; while some say on the tenth day before the Kalends of April.

**50,1,9a** These too have fallen away from what is set before us.

**50,1,9b** And I am afraid to draw out my discourse at very great length concerning these too; for we have much to say.

**50,2,1** For Moses the lawgiver, having completed the whole law, received the command from God and sets down in the last book, that is, Deuteronomy, all the curses — not only concerning the Passover, but also concerning circumcision and concerning tithing and offerings;

**50,2,2** so that, if they have escaped one curse, they have impaled themselves on many; for they will be found accursed for not being circumcised, accursed for not paying tithes, and they become accursed for not making offerings at Jerusalem.

**50,2,3** And O, the men who hurl themselves into many frivolities; for truly one may cite that wise saying of Ecclesiastes, made plain to us by the Holy Spirit: “This Ecclesiastes came to know, that God made man wise and upright, but they sought out for themselves many ways”.

**50,2,4a** For in what respect does their reasoning not fall to the ground?

**50,2,4b** For first, should they celebrate the Passover on the fourteenth, they must take the sheep from the tenth day and guard it till the fourteenth, and then the fast will no longer be of one day but of five: the tenth and the eleventh and the twelfth and the thirteenth and the fourteenth;

**50,2,5** and if the Passover is sacrificed towards evening, the same fourteenth, as it dawns, completes six days in the fast; and the fast will no longer be of one day, and their inquiry about one day has fallen to the ground, since it is not a single day at all.

**50,2,6a** For the original patterns are yoked together, and they have suffered the loss of no small part of what God has ordered.

**50,2,6b** For Christ had to be sacrificed on the fourteenth day according to the law, so that the light which gave them light according to the law might cease among them, when the sun had risen and covered the brightness of the moon.

**50,2,7** For from the fourteenth onwards the visible part of the moon wanes; so too in the law, from the coming and the passion of Christ the Jewish synagogue was darkened, and the gospel shone forth, the law not being abolished but fulfilled, the type not being done away with but presenting the truth.

**50,2,8** For so too, when they had kept the Passover at Jericho, the divine scripture at once added, saying, “And the sons of Israel celebrated the Passover and ate in Gilgal, and the manna failed”,

**50,2,9** bearing witness to them besides and prophesying that at the passion of the Lord, because of their denial of God, the angelic and heavenly food, which among them is called manna, would cease among them.

**50,3,1** But the holy church of God, gathering from every side the bond of what this sacred rite requires, does not fall away from the truth.

**50,3,2** For it makes use not only of the fourteenth, but also of the week that comes round in its cycle [in the] sequence of the sabbath’s seven days, so that, in accordance with what was done by the Lord, the resurrection and the feast may be [as] according to the original pattern.

**50,3,3** And it makes use not only of the fourteenth of the moon, but also of the course of the sun, so that we may not hold two Passovers within one year and then in the next not complete even one.

**50,3,4** Therefore we observe the fourteenth, but we pass beyond the equinox and bring the end of the fulfilment to the holy Lord’s day; and we take the sheep from the tenth, recognizing [the] name of Jesus through the iota, so that nothing may escape us of all that accords with the whole truth of this life-giving [...] of the Passover, the observance of the church, the true observance.

**50,3,5** But I shall pass by this too, the puffing of the baion, that is, of the bloated snake, having sealed myself with the power of Christ and directing my mind to those that follow, calling as usual on God for help.

### Sect 51: Alogi

Against the sect that rejects John’s Gospel and his Apocalypse, the thirty-first, but the fifty-first in the series.

**51,1,1** Next in order after these heresies, after the one named after the Phrygians and the Quintillianists and those called Quartodecimans, another heresy sprang up in the world, like a feeble reptile, unable to withstand the smell of dittany, or of storax, or of rosemary, or of southernwood, or the smell of bitumen, or of incense, or of the jet-stone, or of the deer’s horn.

**51,1,2a** For those who have had experience of these things say that these kinds drive off venomous reptiles; and some call dittany tiktamnos, from the sons of physicians using it to help women in childbirth.

**51,1,2b** So that by us it may fittingly be likened to God the Word, who came from above, from the heavenly places, begotten of the Father timelessly and without beginning.

**51,1,3** For Solomon says of the foolish and worthless woman that “she hates a word of security”; so too these people have hated the solid things of the proclamation, being earthly and hostile to things heavenly.

**51,1,4** Hence, fearing the voice of the Holy Spirit which says, “The voice of the Lord equipping the deer”, [...] of him who said to his own servants and apostles, “Behold, I have given you the authority to tread on serpents and scorpions and on all the power of the enemy”.

**51,1,5** For this was the voice equipping the deer, which was bestowed on the world by means of the holy apostles and evangelists, to tread down the devil’s opposition; and the holy John, [being] one of them, made a stand of the loftiest kind and refuted the power of those who have gone astray and are serpent-shaped.

**51,2,1a** But such people will have no strength in the ark.

**51,2,1b** For the holy Noah is charged, according to the word of the Lord, to make it secure, as he says to him, “You shall pitch it with bitumen within and without”, so as to show the form of the holy church of God, which has the power of bitumen, the power that drives away the dreadful and destructive reptilian teachings; for where bitumen is burned as incense, a serpent cannot stand.

**51,2,2** The holy incense of storax, then, has stupefied them, and they refuse its fragrance, and [...] the power of the southernwood, or of rosemary, which has sprung up against the serpent itself and budded forth above its den.

**51,2,3** For where Ebion and Cerinthus and their associates were contentiously preaching Christ as a mere man — I mean in Asia — there the Holy Spirit made this holy herb, or shrub, spring up for the world, which drove away the serpent and broke the tyranny of the devil.

**51,2,4** For there the holy John, at an advanced age, is charged by the Holy Spirit to preach and to bring back those who had wandered from the way; not by compulsion, but with free choice, revealing to those who were persuaded the light of God, the light in the holy teaching of God.

**51,2,5a** But how long am I to draw out my discourse?

**51,2,5b** For indeed, where southernwood springs up, there a serpent no longer remains nor makes its lair; and where there is the true teaching of God, no dwelling of reptilian teaching will prevail, but it will perish utterly.

**51,3,1** The Alogi, then, say — for I put this surname upon them; from now on they shall be so called, and so, beloved, let us give them a name, that is, Alogi.

**51,3,2a** For they held the heresy called [...], which rejects the books of John.

**51,3,2b** Since, then, they do not receive the Word proclaimed by John, they shall be called Alogi.

**51,3,3** Being, then, wholly strangers to the proclamation of the truth, they deny the purity of the proclamation, and accept neither the Gospel of John nor his Revelation.

**51,3,4** Had they received the gospel while rejecting the Revelation, we would say that perhaps they do this out of exactness, not accepting an apocryphal book because of the things said deeply and darkly in the Revelation.

**51,3,5** But since they do not accept at all the books proclaimed by the holy John, it should be plain to anyone that these, and those like them, are the ones of whom the holy John said in the catholic epistles, “It is the last hour, and you have heard that Antichrist is coming; and now, behold, many Antichrists”, and so on.

**51,3,6a** For these people make excuses, being ashamed to speak against the holy John, because they know that he was in the number of the apostles and beloved by the Lord, to whom he worthily disclosed the mysteries and who lay back upon his breast; and they try to overturn these books in another way.

**51,3,6b** For they claim the books are the work not of John but of Cerinthus, and they say that they are not worthy to be in the church.

**51,4,1a** And from their very starting-point they are at once refuted, “understanding neither what they say nor the things about which they make assertions”.

**51,4,1b** For how will writings that speak against Cerinthus be Cerinthus’?

**51,4,2a** For Cerinthus says that Christ is a recent and mere man, whereas John has proclaimed [him] as the Word who always is, and who came from above and was made flesh.

**51,4,2b** From the very outset, then, their vain slander has been refuted, being a foolish thing that does not understand from what quarter it is refuted.

**51,4,3** For they too seem to believe the same things as we do, but since they do not hold fast the sure points of the proclamation dispensed to us by God through the holy John, they will be found refuted in the things they do not know, as to how they lift up their voice against the truth.

**51,4,4** And if they are willing to come to their senses and to understand, they will know; for we do not reject the teachings that come from the Holy Spirit, which are of weight and firmly secured.

**51,4,5a** For they say — against themselves, for I would not say against the truth — that his books are not in harmony with the rest of the apostles.

**51,4,5b** And so they think that they can find fault with the holy and inspired teaching.

**51,4,6a** And what, says he, did he say?

**51,4,6b** That “At the beginning was the Word, and the Word was in God’s presence, and the Word was God”, and that “The Word was made flesh and made his dwelling among us, and we saw his glory, glory as of the only-begotten from the Father,

**51,4,7** full of grace and truth”; and straightway, “John bears witness and has cried out, saying, This is he of whom I told you”, and that “This is the Lamb of God, who takes away the sin of the world”; and next he says, “And those who heard said to him, Rabbi, where are you staying?”

**51,4,8** And at the same time, “On the next day,” he says, “he wished to go out into Galilee, and finds Philip, and Jesus says to him, Follow me”;

**51,4,9** and a little further on after this he says, “And three days later a marriage took place at Cana in Galilee, and Jesus and his disciples were invited to the wedding supper, and his mother was there”.

**51,4,10** The other evangelists, however, state that he passed forty days in the desert, being tempted by the devil, and that he then returned and took the disciples to himself.

**51,4,11** And these people, lacking all awareness, do not know that each evangelist made it his care to speak, in agreement with the others, the things said by them, and at the same time to reveal the things not spoken by them but thus left out; for it was not their own will, but the order and the teaching were from the Holy Spirit.

**51,4,12a** For if they wish to find fault with this, let them be taught that the other three did not start from one and the same point in the sequence of events.

**51,4,12b** For Matthew is the first to begin proclaiming the gospel.

**51,4,12c** For to him it had been entrusted to proclaim the gospel from the beginning, as we have also said about this at length in another heresy; but it will not grieve us to treat of the same things once more, for the establishment of the truth and the refutation of those who have gone astray.

**51,5,1a** This Matthew, then, is counted worthy [to be the first to proclaim] the gospel, as I said, and most justly so.

**51,5,1b** For it was fitting that he who had turned from many sins, who had risen from the tax-office and followed him who came for the salvation of the human race and said, “I did not come to summon righteous men, but sinners, to repentance”, should [first] offer the proclamation of salvation, as an example to us who are to be saved [in like manner] to him who was brought up from the tax-office and turned back from unrighteousness, so that from him men might learn the love for mankind shown in the coming.

**51,5,2** For after the forgiveness of sins he granted him also the raising up of the dead and the cleansing of leprosy, powers of healing and the driving out of demons, so that he might persuade his hearers not by word only, but [might be able] by the very deed as well to proclaim good tidings: to the perishing, that they will be saved through repentance; to the fallen, that they will rise again; and to the dead, that they will be brought to life.

**51,5,3** And Matthew himself writes and proclaims the gospel in Hebrew letters, and he begins not from the beginning, but relates the genealogy from Abraham: “Abraham,” he says, “was the father of Isaac, and Isaac of Jacob”, and the rest, as far as Joseph and Mary.

**51,5,4a** And he sets it out from the beginning, saying, “The book of the origin of Jesus Christ, son of David”, then he says, “son of Abraham”; then, coming to the very point in question, he says, “Now the birth of Jesus Christ took place thus: his mother Mary having been betrothed to Joseph, she was found, before they came together, to be pregnant from the Holy Spirit.

**51,5,4b** And Joseph, being righteous, sought to put her away secretly.

**51,5,5** And behold, the Lord’s angel appeared to him in a dream and said, Do not put away your wife; for that which is begotten in her is of the Holy Spirit.

**51,5,6a** For behold, she will bear a son, and you shall call his name Jesus.

**51,5,6b** He will save his people from their sins.

**51,5,6c** And this,” he says, “came to pass, so that what was spoken by the Lord through the prophet might be fulfilled, which says, Behold, the virgin will be pregnant, and the rest.

**51,5,7** And having risen,” he says, “from sleep, Joseph did so, and took his wife, and did not know her until she had borne her firstborn son, and he called his name Jesus.

**51,5,8a** And when Jesus had been born,” he says, “at Bethlehem in Judaea, in King Herod’s days, behold, magi from the east arrived in Jerusalem, saying, Where is he who has been born king of the Jews?

**51,5,8b** For we saw his star in the east, and we have come to do him homage”.

**51,5,9a** Where, then, is the account of Zacharias, where are the things recounted by Luke?

**51,5,9b** Where is the vision of the angel?

**51,5,9c** Where is the prophecy of John the Baptist?

**51,5,9d** Where is the rebuke of Zacharias, that he should not be able to speak until the things spoken by the angel had come to pass?

**51,5,10a** Where are the things spoken by Gabriel to the virgin?

**51,5,10b** Where is the demonstration of certainty, when Mary answered the angel himself with understanding, asking as she said, “How shall this be, since I do not know a man?”

**51,5,10c** And where the pure explanation given with exactness, when he said to her, “The Lord’s Spirit will come upon you, and the power of the Most High will overshadow you”?

**51,6,1a** What, then, shall we say?

**51,6,1b** Since Matthew did not proclaim the things said by Luke, is the holy Matthew therefore out of harmony with the truth?

**51,6,1c** Or does the holy Luke not tell the truth, since he has said [nothing] about the first things recounted by Matthew?

**51,6,2a** Did not God apportion to each, so that the four evangelists who were to proclaim might each find what to work at, and might preach some matters harmoniously and equally, so as to show that they set out from the same source, while another relates the things left out by each, according as he received from the Spirit his share in proportion?

**51,6,2b** And what are we to do?

**51,6,3** While Matthew proclaims that Mary gave birth in Bethlehem, [and] according to the genealogies in his account from Abraham and David [...] the incarnate divine manifestation of Christ,

**51,6,4** not thus is the holy Mark found speaking of these things; rather, he makes his opening of the gospel from what took place at the Jordan, and says, “Beginning of the gospel, as it stands written in Isaiah the prophet, A voice of one shouting in the desert”, [...].

**51,6,5** So too what was set down by the holy John, and made secure in the Holy Spirit, took as its care to speak not only of what had already been proclaimed many times, but of the proclamations necessarily left over by the others for him.

**51,6,6a** For the whole plan of the gospels had this character.

**51,6,6b** For when Matthew had proclaimed Christ as born and conceived of the Holy Spirit, [and] brought into being according to the flesh from the seed of David and Abraham, an error arose among those who had not understood (the gospel not being the cause of their going astray, but their own mind having gone astray) the things that had been conceived from the gospel, with good intent, for the establishment of full assurance concerning these same matters.

**51,6,7** For from this source the followers of Cerinthus and Ebion held him to be a mere man, and [...] Merinthus and Cleobius, or Cleobulus, and Claudius and Demas and Hermogenes, who loved the present age and forsook the way of truth.

**51,6,8** For, contradicting the Lord’s disciples at that time, they tried to establish their own folly from the genealogy according to Abraham and David — not thinking rightly, but hunting out their pretext from that quarter.

**51,6,9** For they were often contradicted by Saint John and those about him, Leucius and many others; but shamelessness, having worn its own forehead smooth, made it its ambition to draw its own evils upon itself.

**51,6,10** Immediately after Matthew, Mark, who had become a follower of Saint Peter in Rome, is entrusted with setting out the gospel, and when he has written it he is sent by Saint Peter into the country of the Egyptians.

**51,6,11** This man happened to be among the seventy-two who had dispersed at the saying the Lord uttered, “Unless someone eats my flesh and drinks my blood, he is unworthy of me” — as the account [would be] plain to readers of the gospels; yet, having come back through Peter, he is counted worthy to preach the gospel, being filled with the Holy Spirit.

**51,6,12** He begins to preach from the point at which the Spirit bade him, setting his beginning in Tiberius Caesar’s fifteenth year, thirty years later than the period Matthew treats.

**51,6,13** When a second evangelist had arisen and had not clearly declared the descent of God the Word from above — expressively, indeed, in every respect, yet not with such great exactness — there came upon the deceived persons already mentioned a second darkening of their thoughts, so that they were not counted worthy of the enlightenment of the gospel,

**51,6,14** since they said: See, here is a second gospel too, which tells about Christ and nowhere speaks of his birth from above; but, it says, at the Jordan the Spirit came down upon him, and [...] a voice: “This is my Son, the beloved, on whom my good pleasure rests.”

**51,7,1** Since these things were thus being brought about among such senseless people, the Holy Spirit compels and spurs Saint Luke to lift up the minds of the deceived as from the lowest depth, and to take in hand once more what had been left out by the others.

**51,7,2** [And] so that none of those led astray might suppose that he had described the birth in a fabulous manner, he carries the matter back to what went before, and for the sake of exactness he goes through the whole account in detail,

**51,7,3** and for the demonstration of the truth he brings in as witnesses those who had become ministers of the word, saying, “Inasmuch as many have undertaken”, in order to show certain people up as undertakers — I mean the followers of Cerinthus and Merinthus and the others;

**51,7,4a** then what does he say?

**51,7,4b** “It seemed good to me too, having closely followed from the start those who became eyewitnesses and servants of the word, to write in sequence to you, most excellent Theophilus” (whether he said this when writing at that time to someone named Theophilus, or to every person who loves God), “[that you may know],” he says, “the certainty of the words in which you were instructed”;

**51,7,5** and he spoke of the instruction as having come first, since others had already given him instruction, but he had not securely learned from them the precise truth.

**51,7,6** Then he says, “In the days of Herod the king there arose, of the course of Abijah the high priest, a priest by the name of Zacharias, and he had a wife from among Aaron’s daughters, whose name was Elizabeth.”

**51,7,7** And he begins earlier than Matthew; for Matthew marked out a span of thirty years from the start, whereas Mark set down what came after [the] thirty years, the events that took place in truth at the Jordan, in agreement with Matthew and Luke.

**51,7,8** Matthew made his narrative begin from a time thirty years before the events at the Jordan and the baptism; but Luke made known the time from six months before the time when the Saviour was conceived in the womb, and again the nine months and a few days of the Lord’s conception, so that the whole time is thirty-one years and more.

**51,7,9** He also narrates the vision of God granted to the shepherds through the angels who brought the good news, and how he was born in Bethlehem and wrapped in swaddling clothes in a manger and circumcised on the eighth day, and how after forty days they performed the offering for him according to the law, and how Simeon received [him] in his arms, and how Anna the daughter of Phanuel gave thanks, and how he departed to Nazareth, and how every year he went up to Jerusalem with his parents, who performed for him what the law required — none of which has been treated by Matthew, nor by Mark, nor indeed by John.

**51,8,1** Hence also certain others from among the Greek philosophers — I mean Porphyry and Celsus and Philosabbatius, that terrible and deceitful serpent who sprang from the Jews — in setting out their overthrow of the gospel account, accuse the holy evangelists, being natural and fleshly men, waging war after the flesh and unable to please God, and not having understood the things [spoken] through the Spirit.

**51,8,2** For each [of them], stumbling against the words of truth because ignorance had blinded him within, and falling into this, they said: How can the same day, that of the birth in Bethlehem, itself also hold the circumcision after eight days, and the going up to Jerusalem after forty days, and the things performed for him by Simeon and Anna,

**51,8,3** when on the night on which he was born an angel appeared to him, it says, after the arrival of the magi who had come to do him homage and had opened their bags and presented their gifts, as it says, “An angel was seen by him, saying, ‘Rise up, take your wife and the child, and journey into Egypt, for Herod is seeking the child’s life’”?

**51,8,4a** If, then, on the very night of his birth he was carried off to Egypt and stayed there until Herod’s death, how can he have remained for eight days and been circumcised?
