**51,33,3** For when these people, the Phrygians, had settled there and, like wolves, had snatched away the minds of the simple faithful, they carried the whole city over into their sect, and those who reject the Revelation were at that time taking the field against this teaching, to overthrow it.

**51,33,4** But now, thanks to Christ, at this time, after a period of 112 years, the church is [there] and is growing, [even] if some others happen to be there; but at that time the whole church had been drained off into the sect of the Phrygians.

**51,33,5** That is why the Holy Spirit took pains to reveal to us how the church was going to go astray after the time of the apostles, both John and those who followed in order — a period which was after the Saviour’s ascension [...], over ninety-three years — since the church there was going to go astray and be melted down into the sect of the Phrygians.

**51,33,6** For thus the Lord refutes [them] at once in the Revelation, saying, “Write to the angel of the church that is in Thyatira: These things says he who has his eyes like a fiery flame, and his feet resemble gleaming bronze: I know your works and your faith and love and your service, and that your last are more than the first.

**51,33,7a** But I have this against you, that you let the woman Jezebel, who calls herself a prophetess, lead my servants astray, teaching them to eat things sacrificed to idols and to commit fornication.

**51,33,7b** And I gave her time to repent, and she is unwilling to repent of her fornication.”

**51,33,8a** Do you not see, you people, that he is speaking of the women who are deceived in their conceit of prophecy and who deceive many?

**51,33,8b** I mean Priscilla and Maximilla and Quintilla, whose deceit did not escape the notice of the Holy Spirit,

**51,33,9** but he foretold it prophetically in the mouth of the holy John, who prophesied before his falling asleep, in the days of Claudius Caesar and before, at the time when he was on the island of Patmos; for these people too admit that these things have been fulfilled in Thyatira.

**51,33,10a** So then, it was by prophecy that he was writing to those who were living their lives in Christ there at that time, that a woman was going to call herself a prophetess.

**51,33,10b** And the argument that rises up against the truth, contrived from every quarter, has fallen to pieces, since the word in the Revelation is shown to be prophetic, from the Holy Spirit, in truth.

**51,34,1** And again the same people, hunting after words without skill, are puffed up in their minds, so as to seem to cast out the books of the holy apostle — I mean both the gospel and the Revelation of the same John (and perhaps the epistles as well, for these too are in harmony with the gospel and the Revelation) — and they say, “I saw, and he told the angel,

**51,34,2a** ‘Release the four angels who are at the Euphrates.’

**51,34,2b** And I heard the army’s number: myriads of myriads and thousands upon thousands, and they had put on breastplates fiery and sulphurous and of hyacinth.”

**51,34,3a** For such people supposed that perhaps the truth is [something] laughable.

**51,34,3b** For if it speaks of the four angels who sit at the Euphrates, it is to show the different nations there that sit along the Euphrates, who are Assyrians, Babylonians, and Medes and Persians.

**51,34,4** For these four kingdoms are set out in succession in Daniel, of which the Assyrians reigned first, and the Babylonians in his times, and the Medes succeeded, and after them the Persians, of whom Cyrus was the first king.

**51,34,5** For the nations are placed under angels, as Moses, the holy servant of God, bears witness for me, interpreting the matter in its sequence and saying, “Ask your father and he will tell you, your elders and they will say to you: when the Most High divided the nations, when he scattered the sons of Adam, he fixed the boundaries of nations by the number of God’s angels; and the Lord’s share came to be his people Jacob, and Israel the allotted measure of his inheritance.”

**51,34,6** If, then, the nations are placed under angels, he rightly said, “Release the four angels who are at the Euphrates,” who preside, that is, and are held back from permitting the nations to go to war, until the time of the Lord’s long-suffering, until he commands that through them vengeance be taken for his saints.

**51,34,7a** For the angels set over them are held fast by the Spirit, having no occasion for an onslaught, because they are not yet to unleash the judgement whereby the remaining nations are let loose on account of the outrage against the saints.

**51,34,7b** But such angels are released and come upon the earth, as John prophesies, and the rest of the prophets; for when the angels are set in motion they move the nations to an onslaught of vengeance.

**51,34,8a** And let no one doubt that he was indicating breastplates of sulphur and of fire and of hyacinth.

**51,34,8b** For those nations have their clothing of such a colour.

**51,34,8c** For the sulphur-coloured garments are a certain colour, the so-called quince-yellow wool, and the fiery ones, to mean the scarlet garments, and the hyacinth ones, to indicate the turquoise wool.

**51,35,1** But these people, not having received the Holy Spirit, are judged spiritually, since they fail to understand what is of the Spirit and wish to speak against the Spirit’s words, and do not know the gifts present in the holy church — gifts which the Holy Spirit set forth truly and steadily, with full understanding and a sound mind, as did the holy prophets and the holy apostles,

**51,35,2** among whom the holy John too, through the gospel and the epistles and the Revelation, has imparted to the holy church from the same holy gift.

**51,35,3a** And the saying reaches to them as well, “For the one who blasphemes against the Holy Spirit there will be no forgiveness, either in this age or in the one to come.”

**51,35,3b** For these people have taken the field against the words spoken by the Spirit.

**51,35,4** Now that such and so many things have been said against this sect, thinking that we have said enough, and having trampled it with the power and truth of God like a centipede or the many-footed creeping thing called the millipede, slight in its strength and in the pain of its venom but many-footed and long in shape, let us go on, beloved, advancing again with the power of God to those that follow.

### Sect 52: Adamians

Against the Adamians, the thirty-second, but the fifty-second in the series.

**52,1,1a** There is a creature they call the mole, which makes its den down in the earth, four-footed, breaking up the earth and having its dwelling in its innermost recesses.

**52,1,1b** And everything about the creature is like a small puppy of a dog, having [the same] roundness of size, and it has no sight at all.

**52,1,2** And since the creature is destructive, it roots up from below all the crops of men — above all, all the cucumber beds, and the pungent plantings of onions and garlic, of bulbs and the like, and of lilies and the rest.

**52,1,3** But if, as it breaks through, it happens unawares to come out onto the surface of the earth into the air, or is caught and hunted down by men, it affords something laughable to all who have hunted this creature.

**52,1,4** So I myself too undertake to speak about the sect now [in] hand: how it is blind in heart and senseless, and works desolation for itself and cuts away its own standing from below, and brings about the ruin of many roots, [I mean] of the men who have fallen into it.

**52,1,5** And if it should ever come to be spied out by the intelligent, it provides no ordinary laughter; as the aforementioned creature too is mocked for its blindness [and], for its lack of sight, does not find where to make its way in, so too this sect.

**52,1,6a** For they have given themselves the name of Adam.

**52,1,6b** And we say this having heard it by report from many men; for we have not found it in writings, nor indeed have we come upon any such people.

**52,1,7a** Since many, then, have spoken of it, we thought it right to make mention of it as well.

**52,1,7b** That is why we have also rightly likened it to the aforementioned blind creature, which does not readily come to the sight of men because it is hidden in the earth and works its ruin from below.

**52,1,8a** And it is utterly ridiculous, and I was considering not bringing it into the count at all.

**52,1,8b** But since there is any report of it at all in the world, it would in no way trouble the intelligent listener to be informed about all the tares sown in the world by the devil.

**52,1,9a** For whether such a sect exists or does not exist, since I have heard many speak of it, it has seemed reasonable to me, for safety’s sake, to speak about it too and not to pass it over, even if it has been broken up and no longer exists.

**52,1,9b** For I do not know for certain whether it still exists or does not exist.

**52,1,10a** But why do I stretch out for a long time, using a preface before the narrative?

**52,1,10b** I shall begin to narrate the ludicrous thing, or rather the grievous; for it has the two together, the ludicrous and grief — [grief], in that the devil has conceived in the minds of men how to mock the creature formed by God, and the ludicrous, concerning those who neither see nor have understood anything intelligent.

**52,2,1** For first they say that such people build their churches — or rather their dens and caves (for so I would call the assemblies of the sects) — over hypocausts, and heat them from below, so that there may be warmth for warming those who gather inside in the little building.

**52,2,2** And as they go in, certain keepers of clothes stand at the doors, appointed by them in the manner of cloakroom attendants, and each one going in, whether man or woman, undresses outside and goes in with the whole body naked, as when he came from his mother; and those of them who are reputed to be leaders and teachers all sit as they came from their mothers, some above and some below, mixed together and scattered.

**52,2,3** And all among them are supposedly self-controlled — so they are called and so they boast — and virgins, as they deceive themselves; and they perform their readings and all their other rites while naked.

**52,2,4a** But if anyone should seem — for they say this too — to have fallen into a transgression, they no longer admit him to their gathering; for they say that he [is] the Adam who ate from the tree, and they judge that he is to be thrust out of their church as out of paradise.

**52,2,4b** For they consider their own church to be paradise, and themselves to be Adam and Eve and those with them.

**52,2,5a** For what reason, then, do they heat the little building from below, so that the cold may not touch them?

**52,2,5b** For Adam and Eve did not have their dwelling in a house heated from below, nor were they oppressed by any burning heat, nor did cold weigh upon them.

**52,2,6a** But they had the purest air, measured out by God in good order [with] every right tempering, neither stiffened by the harshness of cold nor laden with the most disagreeable fierceness of burning heat; and the place had been set in order for an ambrosial manner of life, made very [well] by God, filled with delight and well-being, and they were subject neither to frost nor to burning heat, as I said.

**52,2,6b** But since these people are in need of such things, the mockery among them becomes plain.

**52,3,1a** Next let us consider the other point too, how the whole error is refuted: that those were naked not for one hour but continually, “and they were not ashamed.”

**52,3,1b** But for these people the nakedness is not because they are not ashamed, even if they themselves think so, but on account of a pleasure that cannot be sated, which casts its spell on the pupils of the eyes.

**52,3,2** For the modesty praised in all the holy scriptures has been taken away from them, and what was spoken by the prophet has truly been fulfilled, “You had the face of a harlot, you became shameless towards all.”

**52,3,3** And after that hour they put on clothes outside and are no longer Adam; for neither did he and his wife have plenty of clothes at once, but first they sewed fig leaves for themselves, and then tunics of skin were given them, and so, after a long time of their life, “the manifold wisdom of God” put into them the knowledge of garments.

**52,3,4** And in every way these people will be mocked, saying that they are Adam and those with him, lying against themselves and at the same time telling the truth.

**52,3,5** For that these people are not Adam is plain from many proofs, as we have also pointed out; and that they are made sport of by the intelligible serpent is plain through their counterfeit posturing and nakedness and shame and mockery.

**52,3,6a** But it is not worth our while to make much of their refutation.

**52,3,6b** For neither does the creature which they resemble need implements of war or great weapons for its destruction.

**52,3,7a** For such a thing is done away with by a small stick.

**52,3,7b** And often, after being brought up out of its den, it is left alone and perishes by itself, laughed at and mocked and not finding its refuge, just as these people too, when caught, are put to shame through their wholly ridiculous mockery and their indecent way of life and the premise of their drivel.

**52,3,8** And as we are about to examine the next ones, let us again pray the Lord to help us towards the discovery and refutation of the others, and towards the salvation both of ourselves and of our readers.

### Sect 53: Sampsaeans

Against the Sampsaeans, the thirty-third, but the fifty-third in the series.

**53,1,1** Of certain Sampsaeans in Peraea, whom we have already mentioned in the other sects, those indeed who are also called Elchasaites, there is a sect in the land called Peraea, beyond the salt sea, that is, the sea called Dead, [and] in the land of Moab around the brook Arnon, and further on in Ituraea and Nabatitis, as has already been shown by me many times concerning them.

**53,1,2** For they boast that Elchasai is their teacher, and also of two women of his family still living to this day, who are worshipped as goddesses, supposedly because they are of blessed seed.

**53,1,3a** The Ossaeans, Ebionites and Nazoraeans also make use of this book, as I have already repeatedly said.

**53,1,3b** These Sampsaeans spring from it by their very nature; they are neither Christians nor Jews nor Greeks, but, being simply in between, they are nothing.

**53,1,3c** They say they also have another book, called that of Iexai, the brother of Elchasai.

**53,1,4a** They speak of one God and supposedly worship him, making use of certain baptisms; and they are attached to the Jews, though not in all things.

**53,1,4b** And some of them also abstain from things that have life.

**53,1,5a** And they die on behalf of those of the family of Elchasai.

**53,1,5b** And at this time I had heard that one of the women, the one called Marthus, has died, but Marthana still remained even now, unless she too has died.

**53,1,6a** And whenever these women went out, walking somewhere, the crowds following them took the dust of their feet, supposedly for healing, and likewise the spittle of their spittings, and, greatly deluded, used them in phylacteries and amulets.

**53,1,6b** For every error has had first blindness, and then empty talk.

**53,1,7a** And such people accept neither prophets nor apostles, and everything among them is deception.

**53,1,7b** And water is held in honour by them, and they regard it as a god, saying, more or less, that life comes from it.

**53,1,8** And they confess Christ in name, considering him a creature and one who appears time and again; and that he was first formed in Adam, and that he puts off the body of Adam and puts it on again whenever he wishes.

**53,1,9** And that he is called Christ, and that the Holy Spirit is his sister, existing in female form, each of them, both Christ and the Holy Spirit, having a height of ninety-six miles and a breadth of twenty-four; and many other pieces of drivel [...].

**53,2,1** But I have already made the refutations concerning these people many times, in recounting other sects; that is why I did not think it necessary to make much of their overthrow by refutation, since we have already done the work against Elchasai himself, or Elxai, and those derived from him, in the other sects set out before; for such a man, and the sect that comes from him, is detected by all as having gone astray.

**53,2,2a** So then, having struck it like a sun-lizard with the staff of the hope of Christ and of the cross, let us proceed to what follows.

**53,2,2b** For it is fitting to use, in speaking of their counterfeit appellation, the same figure as the name by which these people too have named themselves.

**53,2,2c** For Sampsaeans is interpreted ‘Sun-men’;

**53,2,3a** that is why we have also made mention of the creature.

**53,2,3b** For this lizard is what people call the sun-lizard.

**53,2,3c** But this sect is worse than the lizard, since it has not even the smallest particle of its usefulness.

**53,2,3d** For that creature, when it has grown dim-sighted with age, recovers its sight through the sun’s revolving course; [for] pressing itself into a den that faces the east, and fasting, [and] gazing towards the east, it puts off its dim-sightedness; but this sect, I say, has acquired the creature’s folly in all respects, yet not even the little that is praiseworthy in it.

**53,2,4** So then let this sect too, which we have called a sun-lizard, trampled by the truth, stay where it is, prevailing not at all against the truth of God; and let us, as we said, set out for the others.

### Sect 54: Theodotians

Against the Theodotians, the thirty-fourth, but the fifty-fourth in the series.

**54,1,1** Again there arose a certain Theodotus, an offshoot of the aforementioned Alogian sect, which rejects the Gospel according to John, and God the Word who was in the beginning, [proclaimed] in it, and his Revelation,

**54,1,2a** but who also associated with the other sects mentioned before, and existed alongside them, and succeeded them in time; from him come those called Theodotians.

**54,1,2b** And whether the sect still exists we do not know, but we shall tell what has come to us from writings.

**54,1,3** This Theodotus came from Byzantium, the city now called Constantinople, a leather-worker by trade, but very learned in letters.

**54,1,4** This man, together with a number of others, at a time when a persecution had broken out (I cannot say in which persecution), was arrested by the ruler of the city and examined along with the others for Christ’s sake; and all the others, servants of God, carried off the victory and won heavenly prizes, having borne witness for Christ,

**54,1,5** but this man denied Christ and, falling away from the goal of the truth, went off into a deviation; and, being reproached by many, out of great shame he fled from his homeland, went up to Rome and stayed there.

**54,1,6** But being recognised by the people in Rome, he again fell under the same reproach there, because he was accused by those who knew him on account of his great learning, in that, being a very learned man, he had fallen away from the truth.

**54,1,7a** And by way of a supposed defence of himself, a poor one, he devised this new doctrine, saying, “It was not God I denied, but a man I denied.”

**54,1,7b** Then, being asked, “What man?”, he answered, saying, “I denied Christ, a man.”

**54,1,8** From this point he laid down this doctrine for himself, and so did the Theodotians, who were established from him, saying that Christ is a mere man and was born of the seed of a man.

**54,1,9a** Then, for his poor defence of himself, he gathered together whatever useful texts he found (not because he believed them sincerely, but heaping them up for himself as a pretext for his own deviation): “Because,” he says, “Christ said, ‘But now you seek to kill me, a man who has spoken the truth to you.’”

**54,1,9b** You see, he says, that he is a man.

**54,2,1a** But the wretched man did not recognise that in the same place the Lord says, “the truth which I heard from my Father.”

**54,2,1b** And he calls God his Father; for he does not call a man so.

**54,2,2** For if he had heard it from a man, he would not be glorying in the testimony of the truth in claiming to have heard the truth from men; but he says it to signify that he is God, begotten from the Father above, and made man for our sake and put to death in the flesh, while ever living in his Godhead.

**54,2,3** Then he says that he did not even commit a sin in denying Christ, “since Christ himself,” he says, “said, ‘Every blasphemy will be forgiven men,’” and, “‘He who speaks a word against the Son of Man will have it forgiven him; but he who blasphemes against the Holy Spirit will not have it forgiven him.’”

**54,2,4** And the pitiable man does not know that the Lord, wishing to secure beforehand the salvation of those who had once blasphemed him and were returning to repentance, so as not to fix a condemnation upon them, foretells the saying, out of the surpassing greatness of his gentleness and love for mankind,

**54,2,5** and because he knew that some were going to rise up and blaspheme the Holy Spirit, and define it as of the rank of a servant, and created, and foreign to the essence of God.

**54,2,6** That is why, making it doubly secure, he says, “But for the one who blasphemes against the Holy Spirit there will be no forgiveness, either here or in the age to come” — not praising those who blaspheme him, but showing his foreknowledge and his love for mankind, which secures beforehand the salvation of those who repent of their blasphemy against him.

**54,2,7** For he himself says again, “Whoever denies me before men will be denied before my Father,” and again, “I will deny him,” and again, “Whoever confesses me, I will confess him before my Father.”

**54,3,1a** And again the same Theodotus says, “The law too said concerning him, ‘The Lord will raise up for you a prophet from among your brothers, like me; hear him.’

**54,3,1b** But Moses was a man; so the one raised up from God was Christ, he says, but a man, since he was from among them, just as Moses too was a man.”

**54,3,2** And Theodotus, who has fallen into transgression, does not know within himself how, again, each expression has its own safeguard;

**54,3,3** for that the Lord raised him up from among the brothers is because he was born of Mary, as it says, “See, the virgin will conceive in her womb and will bear a son”; and while remaining a virgin she “will conceive in her womb” (not of the seed of a man), “and will bear a son” (plainly the offspring in the flesh born of a virgin), yet “they will call his name Emmanuel, which is interpreted, God with us.”

**54,3,4** For he is both God and man: God, begotten from the Father without beginning and timelessly, and man, from Mary, because of his presence in the flesh.

**54,3,5** Then he says, “The gospel itself, too, told Mary, ‘The Lord’s Spirit will come upon you,’ and it did not say, ‘The Lord’s Spirit will come to be in you.’”

**54,3,6a** That is why the senseless man, contending on every side, falls away from the truth.

**54,3,6b** For scripture, securing our life on every side so as to show the Trinity in every way and in every form united with itself and working together, and so that none may speak as many indeed do, putting forward evil pretexts for themselves and estranging the Holy Spirit from Christ and [from] the Father — for this reason the angel says to

**54,3,7** Mary, “The Spirit of the Lord will come upon you, and the power of the Most High will overshadow you,” and then afterwards, “Therefore also the holy one who is born will be called Son of God.”

**54,3,8** And he did not say only “the one who is born,” but “therefore also the one who is born,” to show [the] God the Word from above, both come to be in the womb and having fashioned for himself his own incarnation according to his own good pleasure; and on account of his own incarnation, which he dispensed for our salvation, he adds, “Therefore also the holy one who is born will be called Son of God.”

**54,3,9** For if he had said, “The Holy Spirit will come into you,” one could no longer have supposed the Son of God to have arrived in the flesh, but rather the Holy Spirit to have arrived in the flesh.

**54,3,10a** And that it is the Word who has come from above, John, making plain the angel’s gospel message, says, “The Word was in the beginning, and the Word was in God’s presence, and the Word was God.

**54,3,10b** All things came to be through him, and without him not even one thing came to be.”

**54,3,11** Then after this, “And the Word became flesh”; and he did not state, “The Spirit became flesh,” nor yet, “Christ was born a man.”

**54,3,12a** For the divine scripture, safeguarding the matter in every way, recognises him as God and man — God who has come from God above, and man born of Mary without the seed of a man.

**54,3,12b** Whoever steps outside these two true things is not of the truth.

**54,4,1** Again the wretched Theodotus puts forward a pretext, saying, “Jeremiah too said of him, ‘A man he is; and who shall know him?’”

**54,4,2a** And he who has estranged himself from the truth does not know that, as I said, each expression has its own force of meaning.

**54,4,2b** For one who is a man is certainly known by the many who know him — I mean father and mother, brothers and kinsfolk, those who share his dwelling and neighbours, fellow citizens and servants.

**54,4,3** But here scripture, recounting the wonder of his whole work, said “man” on account of the presence in the flesh, but by “who will know him?” it indicated his incomprehensible Godhead.

**54,4,4** For since “no one knows the Son except the Father, and no one knows the Father except the Son and he to whom he reveals him,” no one, then, will know him unless [he himself] reveals it to him.

**54,4,5** And he reveals through the Holy Spirit to his servants his own Godhead and glory and that of his Father, and his eternal life to come, and his mysteries and teaching, and his true presence in the flesh for our sake: that he is God from above and man from Mary.

**54,5,1** Then the same Theodotus says again, “Isaiah too said of him that he is a man, speaking thus: ‘A man who knows how to bear infirmity; and we saw him in affliction and in ill-treatment, and he was dishonoured and was not esteemed.’”

**54,5,2a** But the brutish man does not know how he is refuted once more.

**54,5,2b** For in the very same words Isaiah spoke thus: “Like a sheep he was led to slaughter, and like a lamb that is silent before the one shearing it, so he does not open his mouth; in his humiliation his judgement was taken away.”

**54,5,3** Then he says, “Who will recount his generation, because his life is taken away from men?”, and he did not say, “Life is taken away [from] him,” but “from men.”

**54,5,4a** For the Word is ever living and existing, and has life from himself, and gives life to those who have loved him; his life was taken away from men, [...] he being life from himself.

**54,5,4b** For “the Word is living,” and supplies life to all who have set their hope on him in truth.

**54,5,5a** And the words “Who will recount his generation?” [...].

**54,5,5b** If he was a mere man born of Mary, then his birth was easy to recount.

**54,5,5c** But seeing that he exists before David [and] before Abraham —

**54,5,6** for “Abraham your father,” he says, “longed to see my day, and saw, and rejoiced”; then, when they marvelled and said, “You are not yet fifty years old, and have you seen Abraham?”, refuting Theodotus and the unbelieving, God-denying Jews, he said, “Truly, truly, I say to you, before Abraham I am”;

**54,5,7** for in truth he was before Abraham and before Noah, and before Adam, and before the world, and before heaven, and before the time of the world, and before all created things, being timeless —

**54,5,8a** that is why he is also proclaimed as inexpressible by the Holy Spirit through Isaiah: “Who will recount his generation?

**54,5,8b** For his life is taken away from the earth.”

**54,5,9** But, he says, “The apostles said, ‘a man shown forth to you with signs and wonders,’ and did not say, ‘God shown forth.’”

**54,5,10** But you are refuted again, Theodotus, because the same apostles again said, in those same Acts — as the blessed Stephen says — “See, I behold heaven opened wide, and the Son of Man standing at God’s right hand.”

**54,6,1** And again he puts forward a pretext, saying, “The apostle said of him, ‘the mediator of God and men, the man Christ Jesus,’”

**54,6,2a** and he does not know how he rises up against himself once more.

**54,6,2b** For the one who said, “the mediator of God and men, [the man] Christ Jesus,” himself made it plain, saying, “foreordained Son of God with power, after the Spirit of holiness, from the resurrection of the dead: Jesus Christ our Lord,” and once more, “born of a woman, born under the law.”

**54,6,3** And making these expressions secure he says, “For even if there are many so-called gods and many lords, yet for us there is one God, from whom all things are, and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom are all things [and we unto him].”

**54,6,4a** But if “through whom are all things, and we unto him,” then the Only-begotten would not be a mere man, [existing] from Mary and from then on, nor from the seed of a man.

**54,6,4b** For how could all things be through him — the things that came to be before him, by your account — if he was a mere man?

**54,6,4c** Or how would all the things that were known and came to be before him be unto him?

**54,6,4d** And on every side this man’s drivel has collapsed.

**54,6,5** Having told, in this same discussion, both what is known about him and the refutation point by point, we shall pass on, according to the custom of the sequence; and having struck and slain, as it were, some part of a reptile still writhing, with the truth’s hope and faith, let us examine the next ones [too] and hasten to look over the most beastly sects.

### Sect 55: Melchizedekians

Against the Melchizedekians, the thirty-fifth, but the fifty-fifth in the series.

**55,1,1** Others again call themselves Melchizedekians, having perhaps broken off from those called Theodotians.

**55,1,2a** These glorify the Melchizedek spoken of in the scriptures, considering him a great power.

**55,1,2b** And they say, in their error, that he is on high, in places that cannot be named, and [truly] is not only a certain power but even greater than Christ.

**55,1,3a** And Christ, they consider, simply came and was deemed worthy of that one’s order, supposedly on the strength of the saying, “You are a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek”; so that he is, he says, inferior to Melchizedek.

**55,1,3b** For had he not been placed in some secondary rank, he would not have stood in need of that one’s order.

**55,1,4** And concerning Melchizedek himself they say that he [was] “without mother, without father, without genealogy,” wishing to establish this from the epistle of the holy Paul to the Hebrews.

**55,1,5** And they also fabricate spurious books for themselves, deceiving themselves.

**55,1,6a** But their refutation comes from the very sayings themselves.

**55,1,6b** For as David, prophesying, says of the Lord that he is appointed a priest after the order of Melchizedek, so in the same passage the divine scripture relates that Christ will be a priest.

**55,1,7a** And at once it is found saying [...] “made like the Son of God, he remains a priest for ever.”

**55,1,7b** But if he is likened to the Son of God, he is not the equal of the Son of God.

**55,1,8a** For how can the servant be equal to the master?

**55,1,8b** For Melchizedek was a man, and “without father, without mother” is said not on the ground that he lacked a father or a mother, but because they have not been explicitly named in the divine scripture.

**55,1,9a** For the deepest things of the divine scripture, glorious and surpassing the understanding of human nature, have led many into fancies.

**55,1,9b** For those in Petra of Arabia, which is called Rokom and Edom, marvelling at Moses for his divine signs and having once fashioned his image to worship it, worshipped it, led astray — not that the righteous man became the cause of it for them, but their error, by means of the righteous man, gathered for them in their ignorance a fancy of the matter.

**55,1,10** And in Sebasteia, once called Samaria, having made a goddess of the daughter of Jephthah, they still hold a festival for her every year.

**55,1,11** So these people too, hearing the glorious and wise words of scripture, have turned them to folly, and, carried away into an overweening frame of mind, have left the way of truth; and, having fabricated myths for themselves, they will be refuted on every point.

**55,2,1a** For indeed among some writers Melchizedek’s father and mother are recorded; but this is not found in the express and canonical scriptures.

**55,2,1b** And some have said that his father was called a certain Heraclas, and his mother Astarth, who is also called Astoriane.

**55,2,1c** And he was descended from one of the sons of the natives of that time who dwelt in the plain of Shaveh.

**55,2,2a** And the city was called Salem, concerning which one writer has given out one account and another another.

**55,2,2b** For some say that it is what is now called Jerusalem, once known as Jebus, while others have said that there is another Salem, in the plain of Shechem, opposite the city now called Neapolis.

**55,2,3a** So whether it is this one or that one ([for] the places are not far apart from one another), the narrative nevertheless makes the deed plain.

**55,2,3b** For “he brought out,” it says, “to Abraham bread and wine, and he was priest of the Most High at that time,” he who blessed Abraham and received a tenth from him.

**55,2,4** For it was necessary that the priest of the Most High be honoured by a servant of God, and that Abraham first make an offering to the priest serving in uncircumcision, since from Abraham himself the priesthood was going to arise out of the circumcision, so that “every height lifted up against the knowledge of God” might be brought low,

**55,2,5** so that the circumcision, boasting of the priesthood, might not be able to speak against the priesthood of the holy church of God, which does not observe circumcision or uncircumcision of the flesh, but has the greater and most perfect circumcision, the washing of regeneration.

**55,2,6** For if Abraham offered a tenth to Melchizedek, and those descended from Abraham make offerings to Levi and to Aaron, and yet after a time, when the priesthood was once more from the circumcision through Aaron and his sons in succession, scripture says through David (twelve generations after the birth of Levi, and seven from the succession of Aaron) that the priesthood is established in Melchizedek, it has plainly shown that the priestly order does not abide in the old priesthood of the circumcision;

**55,2,7** for it was being transferred to the priesthood that is before Levi and before Aaron, after the order of Melchizedek, which is now exercised in the church, from the Lord’s presence in the flesh until now, the seed no longer being chosen by succession, but the pattern of virtue being sought.

**55,3,1** For the first priesthood, in uncircumcision, is exercised through Abel, and next also through Noah; and the third through Melchizedek, who served as priest to God not in circumcision but in the righteousness of perfection and in virtue, though in uncircumcision of the flesh.

**55,3,2a** And that Melchizedek was a man, the holy apostle of God himself will make plain in the epistle.

**55,3,2b** For he says thus, “But he whose descent is not reckoned from them has received tithes from the patriarch.”

**55,3,2c** It is plain, then, that his descent is not reckoned from them, but is reckoned from others.

**55,3,3a** And how many others have no genealogy given in the plainest manner?

**55,3,3b** Daniel, Shadrach, Meshach, Abednego, Elijah the Tishbite; for in all the express books the fathers or the mothers of these are nowhere recorded.

**55,3,3c** But so that no error may arise because of this, it will do no harm to tell what we have also learned in traditions.

**55,3,4a** For we have found that Daniel’s father was called a certain Sabaan.

**55,3,4b** And of Elijah too we have in fact found the line of descent from the patriarchs, which I shall also set out in order, thus:

**55,3,5** Elijah the Tishbite was a brother of Jehoiada the priest, being himself too, as it seems, of priestly stock, and the son of Achinaam; and Achinaam the son of Zadok, and Zadok of Ahitub, of Amariah; Amariah the son of Razaza, Razaza of Aazia, Aazia of Phinehas, Phinehas the son of Eleazar, Eleazar the son of Aaron — the priest, that is; Aaron the son of Amram, Amram of Kohath, Kohath of Levi, Levi the third son of Jacob; and Jacob the brother of Esau and the child of Isaac, Isaac the son of Abraham.

**55,3,6a** But in the express books the genealogies of these are not set out at all clearly, except only parts of the account concerning Elijah in the Chronicles.

**55,3,6b** But in fact, of the three youths, Shadrach, Meshach, Abednego, we have found the fathers neither in traditions nor in apocrypha.

**55,3,7a** What then shall we say?

**55,3,7b** Will Shadrach, Meshach, Abednego and their companions also lead us into fancies, so that we reckon what we ought not and marvel extravagantly beyond measure at each narrative, reckoning them fatherless and motherless?

**55,3,7c** But perish the thought!

**55,3,8** For boundaries have been set for us, and foundations for the building up of the faith — both traditions of apostles and holy scriptures and successions of teachers — and on every side the truth of God has been made secure; and let no one be deceived by empty myths.

**55,4,1a** But I will come back again to the matters before us, to the fancies these people have about Melchizedek.

**55,4,1b** For that the righteous man was holy, and priest of God, and king of Salem, is plain; but he was not of the [order] that is in heaven, nor had he come down from heaven;

**55,4,2** for “no one has gone up into heaven except the one who came down out of heaven, the Son of Man,” so says the holy and truthful God the Word.

**55,4,3** And the divine scripture, in proclaiming the order of Melchizedek, and the Spirit, teaching expressly, showed the transfer of the priesthood from the old synagogue and the [...] nation to a nation most excellent and supreme, and not united by a fleshly sharing in succession.

**55,4,4a** For this holy Melchizedek had no succession after him, nor yet any removal from the priesthood.

**55,4,4b** For he himself remained priest the whole time of his life, and is still celebrated in scripture as priest, no one having succeeded him or overthrown the priesthood during the time of his ministry.

**55,4,5** So too our Lord, not being a man but the holy God the Word of God, the Son of God begotten without beginning and without time, ever being with the Father, yet having become man for our sake from Mary, and not from the seed of a man, offers the priesthood [...] to the Father, having taken the lump from humanity, so that he might be appointed priest on our behalf according to the order of Melchizedek, the order that has no succession.

**55,4,6** For he remains for ever offering the gifts on our behalf, having first offered himself through the cross, so as to put an end to every sacrifice of the old covenant, having performed the more perfect and living sacrifice on behalf of the whole world;

**55,4,7** himself the victim, himself the offering, himself the priest, himself the altar, himself God, himself man, himself king, himself high priest, himself the sheep, himself the lamb, having become all things in all for our sake, so that life might come to us in every way, and that he might make the unchangeable foundation of his priesthood stand for ever, no longer apportioning it by seed and by successions, but granting that it be kept, according to the ordinance, in the Holy Spirit.

**55,5,1a** And others again have their fancies, saying [other things] about this Melchizedek.

**55,5,1b** For such people have made their judgement in a fleshly way, not having understood spiritually what the holy apostle said in the same epistle, the one to the Hebrews.

**55,5,2** And Hieracas, the Egyptian heresiarch, holds this Melchizedek to be the Holy Spirit on account of the words “made like,” it says, “the Son of God, he remains a priest for ever” —

**55,5,3a** as though on the strength of the expression which the holy apostle used, “But the Spirit intercedes on our behalf with groans beyond utterance.”

**55,5,3b** But the one who is acquainted with the Spirit’s mind knows that it is on behalf of the elect that he pleads with God.

**55,5,3c** But this man too has fallen wholly away from the matter in hand.

**55,5,4** For the Spirit never put on flesh; and not having put on flesh, he was unable to be king of Salem and a priest of any place.

**55,5,5** But at the time when I make the refutation of this Hieracas and of his sect, I shall then relate these things at length; for now I shall take up the sequence.

**55,6,1a** And how many fancies others too entertain about this Melchizedek!

**55,6,1b** For the Samaritans suppose that he is Shem, the son of Noah.

**55,6,2a** But they too will be found ridiculous.

**55,6,2b** For the divine scripture, securing everything with good order, has fortified the truth in every respect, not setting out the times to no purpose, and numbering the years of the life and succession of each of the fathers.

**55,6,3** For when Abraham was eighty-eight or even ninety years old, more or less, Melchizedek met him and brought out bread and wine for him, prefiguring the enigmas of the mysteries:

**55,6,4** antitypes of [the body of the Lord, as] our Lord [himself] declares, “I am the living bread,” and antitypes of his blood, which poured from his side for the purifying of the defiled and for the sprinkling and salvation of our souls.

**55,6,5a** Now Terah, Abraham’s father, begot Abraham in his seventieth year, and the total comes to a hundred and sixty years, more or less.

**55,6,5b** And Nahor begot Terah when he was seventy-nine years old, and it comes to 239 years.

**55,6,5c** And Serug begot Nahor when he was 130 years old, and the years come to 369.

**55,6,6a** And Reu begot Serug when he was 132 years old, and it comes to the five hundred and first year.

**55,6,6b** And Peleg begot Reu at 130 years of age, and it comes to six hundred and thirty-one years.

**55,6,6c** And Eber begot Peleg in the 134th year of his life, and the years come to seven hundred and sixty-five.

**55,6,7a** And Shelah begot Eber when he was passing the hundred and thirtieth year of his age, and it comes to eight hundred and ninety-five years.

**55,6,7b** And Cainan begot Shelah in the 109th year of his life, and it comes to one thousand and four years.

**55,6,7c** And Arphaxad, being 135 years old, begot Cainan, and it comes to 1,139 years.

**55,6,8** And Shem, in the 102nd year of his life — he who was mentioned before and who is fancied by the Samaritans to be Melchizedek — begot Arphaxad; and altogether it comes to one thousand two hundred and forty-one years, up to the time of Abraham at which he encountered Melchizedek when returning from the slaughter of the kings Amraphel and Arioch and Chedorlaomer and Tidal and those with them.

**55,6,9a** But Shem did not live so many years, as in the drivelling fancy of these people; rather, he begot Arphaxad when he was 102 years old, in the second year after the flood.

**55,6,9b** “And after this he lived,” as the divine scripture says, “500 years, and begot sons and daughters, and died.”

**55,6,10a** So then, since he lived six hundred and two years and died, how could he reach one thousand two hundred and forty-one years, so that Shem son of Noah, who came ten generations before Abraham, should be called Melchizedek by these people ten generations later, and one thousand two hundred and forty-one years later?

**55,6,10b** And oh, the great error of men!

**55,6,11a** But by the reckoning of other copies, from Shem’s age — from the time when Shem himself was born — up to the aforementioned time of Abraham’s meeting with Melchizedek, which was in the eighty-eighth or the ninetieth year of his life, it comes to six hundred and twenty-eight years, more or less, so that Shem could by no means reach the aforementioned time of Abraham, so as to be thought Melchizedek as well.

**55,6,11b** And the vulgar chatter of the Samaritans too has collapsed on every side.

**55,7,1** And the Jews, again, say that such a man was righteous and good and priest of the Most High, as the divine scripture has it, but that because he was the son of a harlot, they say, his mother has not been written down, nor is his father known.

**55,7,2a** But the drivelling argument of these people too has collapsed.

**55,7,2b** For Rahab too was a harlot and is written down, and Zimri, who committed fornication, is written, and Cozbi with him, although she was a foreigner and did not trace her descent from the race of Israel.

**55,7,2c** [...] And “everyone who does not enter by the door,” as the holy gospel said, “is a robber and is not a shepherd.”

**55,7,3a** And in the church, indeed, some define this Melchizedek in various ways.

**55,7,3b** For some suppose that he is actually the Son of God, who appeared to Abraham at that time in the form of a man.

**55,7,4** But they too fall away from the matter in hand; for nobody will ever come to be like himself, as the divine scripture has it, “made like the Son of God, he remains a priest for ever.”

**55,7,5** But in reality “he whose descent is not reckoned from them has received tithes from Abraham”; for since his descent is not reckoned from the Israelites themselves, it is reckoned from other men.

**55,7,6** Having made the list of all these, [whom] we have mentioned because of this sect, we have told about them as in a digression.

**55,8,1** And the aforementioned sect both brings up its offerings in the name of this Melchizedek and says that he is the one who brings men in to God, and through him, it says, one must make offering to God, because he is a ruler of righteousness, appointed by God in heaven for this very purpose, being a certain spiritual being and appointed to the priesthood of God.

**55,8,2** And it is to him, they say, that we must make offerings, so that offering may be made through him on our behalf and we may find life through him.

**55,8,3a** And Christ, it says, was chosen to call us out of many ways into this one knowledge, being anointed by God and made elect, since he turned us away from idols and pointed out the way to us.

**55,8,3b** From him the apostle, having been sent forth, revealed to us that Melchizedek is great and remains priest for ever.

**55,8,4** And consider how great this man is, and that the lesser receives blessing from the greater; and that is why, it says,

**55,8,5** he also blessed Abraham the patriarch, as being greater — he whose initiates we are — so that we ourselves too may obtain the blessing from him.

**55,9,1a** And how vain is the mind of all the sects!

**55,9,1b** For see, these people too have disowned their own Master, the one who “purchased them with his own blood,”

**55,9,2a** who did not begin from Mary, as they themselves suppose,

**55,9,2b** but who is ever with the Father, God the Word, begotten from the Father without time and without beginning, as all Scripture relates; to him the Father also said, “Let us make man after our image and after our likeness”, and not to Melchizedek.

**55,9,3** For even if he did become priest of the most high God in his own generation, and there was no succession after him, yet he is not one who came down from above, from heaven.

**55,9,4a** For Scripture did not say that he “brought down” bread and wine, but that he “brought out” for him and for those with him, as one who meant to welcome the patriarch passing by on his way from the kings;

**55,9,4b** and he blessed him because of the righteousness that was in him and the man’s faithfulness and piety,

**55,9,5** since the patriarch, tested in all things, fell away from righteousness in nothing, but in this matter too had God as his helper against those who had indeed fallen upon the land of the Sodomites and carried off his nephew as well, the holy Lot, whom he also brought back with all the spoil and booty.

**55,9,6a** And from what passage can we not show that [the] Son was ever with the Father?

**55,9,6b** For “the Word was in the beginning, and the Word was in God’s presence, and the Word was God”; but it did not say, “Melchizedek was in the beginning”, nor “Melchizedek was God”.

**55,9,7a** And again, “The Lord came, then, to Abraham; and the Lord rained down from the Lord fire and sulphur upon Sodom and Gomorrah”.

**55,9,7b** And the apostle himself says, “One God, from whom all things are, and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom all things are”.

**55,9,8a** And lest anyone should say: where then is the Spirit, since he speaks of one and of one?

**55,9,8b** It was not fitting for the Spirit to become his own commender; for the divine Scripture always takes care to be a pattern for us.

**55,9,8c** The apostle was giving utterance in the Holy Spirit when he said, “One God, from whom all things are, and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom all things are”.

**55,9,8d** And it was in the Holy Spirit that he was saying this; for it was not so that the Trinity should be left wanting.

**55,9,9a** And the Lord himself says plainly, “Go and baptise all the nations into the name of Father and Son and Holy Spirit”.

**55,9,9b** And again the apostle says, “But one is the Spirit who distributes to each as he wills, for what is profitable”.

**55,9,10a** Here, then, is the Father, here the Son, here the Holy Spirit; and nowhere is it said of Melchizedek that he bestows among the gifts, nor among the heights.

**55,9,10b** And in vain are these false and contrived stumbling-blocks chanted among these people, which have come to them not from the truth but from the very hissing of the dragon, as he has been able to deceive and lead astray every heresy.

**55,9,11** But word has reached us again that some, having strayed further than all those mentioned before and carried away by a still greater arrogance of thought, have dared to turn to perplexity of reasoning, to come to a blasphemous notion, and to assert that this very Melchizedek is the Father of Jesus Christ our Lord.

**55,9,12a** And oh, the unguarded thinking of men, and the heart that is deceived and has no firm standing in the truth!

**55,9,12b** For because the apostle calls Melchizedek fatherless and motherless and without genealogy, such people, led astray in their thinking by the loftiness of the expression, and [...] having conceived the [...] things that correspond to the Father of all, have shaped within themselves an error of blasphemy.

**55,9,13** For because the Father of all, God the Almighty, has neither father nor mother nor beginning of days nor end of life (for this is acknowledged [by] all), they likened Melchizedek to him because the apostle had spoken of him in this way, and have fallen into a blasphemy of folly, not understanding the other things said about him.

**55,9,14a** For concerning Melchizedek it has been said that “he was priest of the Most High”.

**55,9,14b** If, then, this man is the Most High and the Father, it follows that, being priest of another Most High, he would no longer himself be the Father of all, since he serves as priest to another Most High.

**55,9,15a** And oh, the vulgarity of men, which does not perceive what is true but turns itself towards what leads astray!

**55,9,15b** For the holy apostle, gathering together the conclusion of the solution of the whole question, said, “But he whose descent is not reckoned from them” (clearly, but from others) “has taken tithes of Abraham”, and again, “who in the days of his flesh made prayers and supplications”, he says, “to the one able to save him”; and that [the] Father did not put on flesh is clear.

**55,9,16a** But having discussed these matters too sufficiently, let us leave the heresy behind, as though we had struck a shrew-mouse with a stone by means of the firm faith and its foundation, and having escaped its venomous poison.

**55,9,16b** For they say that the shrew-mouse does no harm at the time to the one it bites, but in the course of time wastes the body away and brings the one it has attacked to the disfigurement of leprosy, extending to all his limbs.

**55,9,17** So too this heresy [...] in the mind appears to those who have just heard about these things as nothing [...], but the drawn-out talk of its arguments, as it is rubbed in, produces questionings and, as it were, destruction for those who do not obtain the help of this antidote, the overthrow and rebuttal of it that we have made.

**55,9,18a** And the creature is not quickly seen.

**55,9,18b** For it goes about by night and so does its harm, especially in the land of the Egyptians, so that those who have knowledge of the creature should know that we have made mention of the creature’s nature neither idly nor by false accusation, in likening it to the harm that comes from the heresy; for such is the injury it does.

**55,9,19** Next I shall proceed to the others, so that, having been counted worthy in God to fulfil the promise, we may give thanks to God.

### Sect 56: Bardesianists

Against the Bardesianists, the thirty-sixth, but the fifty-sixth in the series.

**56,1,1a** Next after these follows a certain Bardesanes, so called.

**56,1,1b** This Bardesanes, from whom the heresy of the Bardesianists arose, was by race from Mesopotamia, one of those who dwell in the city of the Edessenes.

**56,1,2a** At first he was a most excellent man, and he composed not a few treatises while he still had a sound mind.

**56,1,2b** For he came from the holy church of God, and was a learned man in the two languages, both the Greek tongue and the speech of the Syrians.

**56,1,3** Having at first been on intimate terms with Abgar, the ruler of the Edessenes, a most holy and most learned man, and having worked together with him and at the same time shared in his learning, he lived on after that man’s death until the times of Antoninus Caesar (not the one called Pius, but Verus).

**56,1,4** He argued a great deal [with] Abidas the astronomer, speaking against Fate, and other compositions of his in keeping with the pious faith are also current.

**56,1,5a** He also stood up against Apollonius, the companion of Antoninus, refusing to deny that he called himself a Christian.

**56,1,5b** And he very nearly attained the rank of a confessor, and gave wise answers, defending piety courageously, saying that he did not fear death, which, [he said], would come of necessity even should he not contradict the emperor.

**56,1,6** And so the man was greatly adorned in every respect, until he fell into the blunder of his own heresy, becoming like a most beautiful ship that takes on an incomparable cargo and is then broken up beside the banks of the harbour, losing all its merchandise and bringing death upon the other passengers as well.

**56,2,1** For this man fell in with the Valentinians to his ruin, and from their wickedness he draws up this poison, tare-like as it is; he too introduced many principles and emanations and, denying the resurrection of the dead, laid down this heresy as doctrine.

**56,2,2** He uses the Law and the Prophets, the Old and the New Testament, and certain apocryphal writings as well.

**56,2,3** But he too will be overthrown, together with all those before him and those after him, since he has estranged himself from the truth and has been changed, as it were, from a bright torch of fire into soot.

**56,2,4** Concerning the resurrection of the dead, indeed, we have already spoken in many heresies; but it will do no harm to use a few words again in the refutation of this man.

**56,2,5** For if you accept the Old Testament, you there, and the New likewise, from what quarter will you not be shown up as perverting the way of the truth and estranging yourself from the life of the Lord in truth?

**56,2,6** For that the Lord himself first, so that he might become for us the pledge of the resurrection and the firstborn from the dead, both died for us and rose again, [is clear],

**56,2,7a** and not merely in appearance did he suffer; for he was buried as well, and they carried his body.

**56,2,7b** And Joseph from Arimathea is a witness, and so are the women who carried ointments to the tomb, and the weight of the hundred pounds of aloes, that it was no appearance and no illusion.

**56,2,8** And the angels who appeared to the women also bear witness: “He has risen. He is not here; why do you look for the living among the dead?”

**56,2,9** And they did not say, “He did not die,” but, “He has risen” — the one who suffered in the flesh, yet lives ever in the spirit and is impassible in his own Godhead, who is ever begotten from above of the Father, who in the last days was pleased to become man of Mary the Virgin, as the holy Paul testifies when he says, “Born of a woman, born under the law”.

**56,2,10a** Have you, then, never yet heard the text that says, “This perishable thing must put on imperishability, and this mortal thing must put on immortality”?

**56,2,10b** Or did Isaiah the prophet not persuade you when he said, “And the dead shall rise, and those in the tombs shall be raised”, and the Lord himself when he said, “And these shall be raised to eternal life, and those to eternal punishment”?

**56,2,11a** Or do you not remember Abel’s converse with God after death, how it says that it was not the soul that pleaded and cried out to God, but the blood?

**56,2,11b** Yet blood is not the soul, but the soul is in the blood.

**56,2,12a** For the blood is a visible body, while the soul, being something invisible, has its place in the blood.

**56,2,12b** And from every side, Bardesanes, your false belief has been refuted, overthrown by the truth itself.

**56,3,1** As for many principles, since I have often said much against those who speak in this way, I do not make a long discussion of it here, but, as in [a digression], I shall call to mind the holy apostle, who says, “For us there is one God, the Father, from whom are all things and we unto him, and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom all things are, and we through him”.

**56,3,2a** How, then, will there be polytheism and many principles, if there is “one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom all things are, and we through him”?

**56,3,2b** So then, the Maker is one, and there are not many gods, nor many aeons.

**56,3,3a** For he said, “If indeed there are many so-called gods”.

**56,3,3b** But the word “so-called” he pronounced as [of] things that do not exist; and because of the Greeks’ so-called gods, whom they deified — sun and moon, stars and things like these — by this pronouncement he shut out the thinking of all who have gone astray.

**56,3,4** And since the sound faith is preserved on every side, being the foundation and the salvation of the faithful, the contrived empty talk of all the heresies has easily been overthrown as well, just as this man too has been overthrown, having made himself pitiable and estranged himself from life.

**56,3,5a** For the prophet says to the holy church of God, “I will make your stone carbuncle, and your foundations sapphire, and your walls choice stones, and your battlements jasper”.

**56,3,5b** Then afterwards he says, “Every voice that shall rise up against you, you shall defeat them all, but against you it shall not prosper”.

**56,3,6** For nothing shall prevail against the faith of the truth, since “it is built upon the rock, and the gates of Hades shall not prevail against it”, as the holy God the Word promised it, its King and Bridegroom and its Lord and Master, to whom be glory, honour and might — to the Father in the Son together with the Holy Spirit — for ever and ever, Amen.

**56,3,7** Now that this heresy too has been trampled underfoot, [...] like the head of a snake that has been cut off and is still writhing, struck by the wood of life, [and] let us ourselves, giving thanks to God, go on again to the others, examining them, beloved.

### Sect 57: Noetians

Against the Noetians, the thirty-seventh, but the fifty-seventh in the series.

**57,1,1** After him again another arose, Noetus by name, not many years ago but about a hundred and thirty years before the present time, more or less, an Asian of the city of Ephesus;

**57,1,2** who, carried along by an alien spirit, wished to say and teach of his own accord things which neither prophets [proclaimed] nor apostles, which neither the church held from the beginning nor anyone ever conceived; he himself, of his own accord, lifted up by an elation of madness, dared to say that the Father suffered;

**57,1,3** and, carried away by a still greater conceit and derangement, he used to say that he himself was Moses and his brother Aaron.

**57,1,4** But meanwhile, because of the report that sounded abroad about him, the blessed presbyters of the church summoned him and questioned him on all these matters, and on whether he had brought forward this blasphemy concerning the Father.

**57,1,5** When he was brought before the presbytery he at first denied it, because no one before him had spewed out this terrible and destructive bitterness.

**57,1,6** But later, having cast some of the madness he had into certain others, so to speak, and drawing some ten men along with him, he was lifted up to greater conceit and insolence, [and], having grown daring, he thenceforth taught the heresy openly.

**57,1,7** Once more the same presbyters summoned both him and the men who had been corrupted along with him, and questioned them again about the same things.

**57,1,8** But he, together with those whom he had led astray, hardening his brow, thenceforth contradicted them openly, saying, “What wrong, then, have I done in glorifying one God? I know one God and no other besides him, who was born, suffered and died”.

**57,1,9a** Since, then, he persisted in these views, they expelled him from the church together with those whom he had made disciples of his own doctrine.

**57,1,9b** He himself died not long ago together with his own brother; he was not buried with glory as Moses was, nor his brother with honour as Aaron was.

**57,1,9c** For they were thrown out as transgressors, and not one of the God-fearing laid them out for burial.

**57,1,10** After this, those whose minds he had corrupted strengthened this doctrine, led on by the same texts as their evil teacher had been from the beginning.

**57,1,11** For when, questioned by the presbytery, he said that he glorified one God, he heard from them, as lovers of the truth: We too glorify one God, but as we know how to glorify him rightly;

**57,1,12a** and we hold one Christ, but as we know one Christ, Son of God, having suffered as he suffered, having died as he died, risen, gone up into heaven, being at the Father’s right hand, coming to judge the living and the dead.

**57,1,12b** These things we say, having learned them from the divine Scriptures, and these things we know.

**57,2,1a** From him, then, the Noetians, being an offshoot of him, and those who come from them hold this doctrine, trying to establish their frenzied teaching from these texts.

**57,2,1b** From what God said to Moses: “I am your fathers’ God; I am first, and I am after these things; you shall have no other gods”, and what follows.

**57,2,2a** So they said: We, therefore, know him alone.

**57,2,2b** If, then, Christ, having come, was born, he himself is Father, he himself Son; so he is [the] same God who [is] ever and has now come;

**57,2,3** as Scripture says, “This is your God; no other shall be reckoned beside him; he found out every way of knowledge and gave it to Jacob his servant and to Israel who is beloved by him; afterwards he was seen upon earth and lived among men”.

**57,2,4** Do you see, he says, again how the divine Scripture instructs us not to consider one God and then another, when it says that God himself is [the] one who alone is, and he himself was seen afterwards?

**57,2,5** And again they use this other text, where it says, “Egypt has laboured, and the commerce of the Ethiopians; and the Sabaeans, men of stature, shall pass over to you and shall be your servants, and shall follow behind you bound in fetters, and shall worship in you and pray in you, for God is in you and there is no God besides you. For you are God, and we did not know it, the God of Israel, the Saviour”.

**57,2,6a** Do you see, he says, how the divine Scriptures signify one God and proclaim that he [became] manifest?

**57,2,6b** And by common consent he is one, ever being the same.

**57,2,7** And for this reason we do not speak of many gods, but of one God, the very same being impassible, himself Father of the Son, himself the Son and the one who suffered, that he might save us by his own suffering, and we cannot speak of another — having learned, forsooth, this confession from their master, and this wicked notion and ruinous madness.

**57,2,8** Then they bring forward other texts to support themselves, as their teacher said, “The apostle too bears witness to these words, saying, ‘Whose are the fathers, from whom is Christ according to the flesh, who is God over all, blessed for ever, Amen’”.

**57,2,9** They choose to give a one-sided account, in this manner like Theodotus: he described him in the most extreme fashion as a mere man by nature, while this man in turn takes the other extreme in a narrow way, holding that the same one, God the Father, and Son and Holy Spirit, suffered in the flesh and was born.

**57,2,10** So neither did those followers of Theodotus say anything in truth, nor this Noetus and those from him — mindless, rather, in reality — just as the divine Scriptures refute both parties and all who have gone astray.

**57,3,1** But to everyone who has his mind directed towards God [...] and has been illumined in the divine Scripture and in the Holy Spirit, their argument will appear readily refuted and full of every folly.

**57,3,2** For it is the work of audacity and blindness, [...] the thinking that says that the same one is Father, and the same one Son, and the same one has suffered; so that those who say such things will be judged even by right and reasonable thinking.

**57,3,3a** For how could the same one be Father while being Son?

**57,3,3b** For if he is Son, he must surely be the son of someone, from whom he has been begotten.

**57,3,4a** But if he is Father, it is impossible for him to beget himself.

**57,3,4b** And again, the one called Son did not beget himself, but has been begotten of the Father.

**57,3,4c** And oh, the great derangement of men and their reasoning that cannot be set straight!

**57,3,5a** For there is, there is a reasonable account — not as these people imagine, but as the truth shows it to us through the divine Scripture.

**57,3,5b** For at once the Lord declares, saying, “Behold, my beloved child shall understand, whom I have chosen, whom my soul has loved; upon him I will put my Spirit”.

**57,3,6** And you see how the voice of the Father shows a Son by nature, upon whom he also puts his Spirit.

**57,3,7a** Then the Only-begotten himself says, “Glorify me, Father, in the glory that I had beside you before the world came into being”.

**57,3,7b** And he who says, “Father, glorify me”, does not call himself Father, but recognises his own Father.

**57,3,8a** And again in another place, “A voice came from heaven: This is my Son, hear him”.

**57,3,8b** And it was not said, “I am my Son; hear me”, nor again, “I have become a son”, but rather, “This is my Son; hear him”.

**57,3,9a** And in saying, “I and my Father are one thing”, he did not say, “I and my Father am a single one”, but “I and my Father are one thing”.

**57,3,9b** And “I and the Father”, with the article “the” and the word “and” standing in between, signifies a Father who is Father by nature and a Son who is Son by nature.

**57,4,1a** And again, concerning the Holy Spirit, “If I go away, that one comes, the Spirit of truth”.

**57,4,1b** And this is very clear indeed: “I go away, and that one comes”.

**57,4,1c** He did not say, “I go away and I come”, but by “I” and “that one” he signified that the Son is subsistent and that the Holy Spirit is subsistent.

**57,4,2** And again, “The Spirit of truth, proceeding from the Father and taking from what is mine”, so as to show the Father as subsistent, the Son as subsistent, and the Holy Spirit as subsistent.

**57,4,3** And again, at the Jordan, the Father was speaking from on high, the Son was going down into the Jordan, and the Spirit, in the very midst, appeared in a dove’s form and came upon him, although the Spirit had not worn flesh nor put on a body.

**57,4,4** But so that it might not be supposed that there is a blending with the Son, the Holy Spirit takes shape in the form of a dove, so that the Spirit might be seen to be by nature subsistent.

**57,4,5a** And from what other source can we not bring proof against these people who have conceived a craving for derangement?

**57,4,5b** For if their thinking is true, and their stale argument, which has neither coherence nor force nor any reasoning or show of understanding, then indeed the Scriptures must be cast out, which everywhere know the Father as Father, the Son as Son, and the Holy Spirit as Holy Spirit.

**57,4,6a** But what do you say, you there?

**57,4,6b** Would those who truly offer reverence to the Trinity, who are sons of the truth and of the sole apostolic and catholic church, hold to polytheism?

**57,4,6c** It is not so.

**57,4,7** For who will not say that there is one God of truth, the Father Almighty, from whom is the only-begotten Son, in truth God the Word, the subsistent Word, begotten of him in truth, without beginning and outside time?

**57,4,8** Therefore the church securely proclaims one God, Father and Son; because “I am in the Father, and the Father is in me, and the two of us are one thing”, that is, a single Godhead and one will and one lordship.

**57,4,9** And from the Father himself the Spirit too proceeds, being subsistent and perfect in truth, the Spirit of truth, who gives light to all things, who receives from the Son, the Father’s Spirit, the Spirit of Christ.

**57,4,10** Therefore the church knows one Godhead: one God of truth — Father, a perfect, subsistent Father; and Son, a perfect, subsistent Son; and Holy Spirit, a perfect, subsistent Holy Spirit — one Godhead, one sovereignty, one lordship.

**57,4,11** Therefore everywhere the divine Scriptures have clearly proclaimed one God, that is, a consubstantial Trinity, ever of the same Godhead, of the same lordship.

**57,4,12a** And from every side the argument of your mindlessness has collapsed, Noetus.

**57,4,12b** So then, these things having been said, and in opposition to the utterances of the aforementioned Noetus, one must examine from the beginning and so overthrow his chief points.

**57,5,1** First, then, since he brought forward the heading, “One God, from whom are all things and we unto him, and one Lord Jesus Christ, because of whom all things are, and we through him”, do you not see how, in saying, “One God, from whom are all things and we unto him”, he signifies the one principle, so that he might not turn our thinking towards many principles and lead back into polytheism the mind of men that had gone astray in the empty babble of polytheism?

**57,5,2a** For you see how he uses one name and one designation, without denying the only-begotten God.

**57,5,2b** For he knows him as Lord and knows him as God; therefore, to make it secure, he says, “and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom are all things”.

**57,5,3** And when he says this of the Lord, he did not indicate that the same one is Father and the same one Son, but showed the Father as being Father and the Son as truly Son,

**57,5,4** since in saying “one God” of the Father he [spoke] not as denying that the Son is God (for if he is not God, neither is he Lord; but since he is Lord, he is also God), but, constrained by the Holy Spirit to bring them under a single designation, the holy apostle expressed the faith to us, plainly signifying one Lord, and so also God.

**57,5,5a** But let no one suppose, because he speaks of one and one, that he has perhaps left out the number of the Trinity, since he did not name the Holy Spirit.

**57,5,5b** For when he named Father and Son, God and Lord, it was in the Holy Spirit that he named them.

**57,5,6** For it was not because he said “one God” of the Father, “from whom are all things”, that he denied that he is Lord; nor again, because he said “and one Lord Jesus Christ”, did he deny that he is God,

**57,5,7** but just as in the case of the Father he was content with the one designation, saying “one God”, while together with the title God it is clear that he is also Lord, so too for the Son he was satisfied with “one Lord”; and together with “Lord” the title God goes with it as well.

**57,5,8a** So in speaking of the Father and the Son he was not empty of the Holy Spirit, but, as I said, he was speaking in the Holy Spirit.

**57,5,8b** And it is ever the custom of the Holy Spirit not to make a commendation of himself [...], lest he give us an example of [...] about ourselves and of our making a commendation [of ourselves] too.

**57,5,9** So then, “one God, the Father, from whom all things are, and one Lord Jesus Christ, through whom all things are”, and the Holy Spirit is one, not alien to God, but subsistent, because he is Spirit of God and Spirit of truth and the Father’s Spirit and Christ’s Spirit.

**57,6,1** But if there is need to speak also of the text, “Egypt has laboured, and the commerce of the Ethiopians; and the Sabaeans, men of stature, shall pass over to you, and shall be your servants, and shall follow behind you bound in fetters, and shall worship in you and pray in you, for God is in you and there is no God besides you; for you are God, and we did not know it, the God of Israel, the Saviour”, he will say, “Do you not see”, he says, “that God is one,

**57,6,2** from all these texts that we have pointed out?” — not understanding what is said, but craftily cutting the Scriptures short, explaining them crookedly and bringing in the words in transposed order; Noetus, or the Noetians who come from him, does not speak consistently or to the point, nor give an account in proper sequence.

**57,6,3** For just as people [give] a bad dog the name Lion, and call those who cannot see at all Keen-sighted, and call gall sweet, and some have named vinegar honey, and some have named the Erinyes the Eumenides,

**57,6,4** so too this man and those from him — he who bears the name of Noetus is mindless, and those from him are without mind, not grasping at all the sequence of what he says and of what they put forward, so that the saying of the holy apostle is fulfilled in them, “understanding neither what they say nor the things about which they make assertions”.

**57,7,1** For see, brethren, how the divine Scripture spoke further above — or rather the Lord himself — as the beginning of the passage has it; from which point indeed one must show, concerning the whole truth and subject of the passage itself,

**57,7,2a** that “Ask me about my sons and my daughters, and give me commands [concerning] the works of my hands. I made”, he says,

**57,7,2b** “the earth and man upon it; I with my hand made firm the heaven; I laid my command on all the stars; I raised up in righteousness a king, and all his ways are straight; he it is who shall build my city and turn back the captivity, not for ransom nor for gifts, said the Lord of hosts”.

**57,7,3** Then next, “Egypt has laboured, and the merchandise of the Ethiopians”, and what follows, “for God is in you”.

**57,7,4a** But in whom should we say, if not in the Father’s own Word?

**57,7,4b** For the Son is truly God the Word, and in him the Father is known, as he says, “He who has seen me has seen the Father”, and “I have glorified your name upon the earth”.

**57,7,5a** Then again, “I raised up a king”.

**57,7,5b** Do you not see that it is the voice proper to the Father that raised up from itself the true Word as King over all, him who was truly begotten of him without beginning and outside time?

**57,7,6** Him he also raised again, the King himself, as the holy apostle says, “But if the Spirit of him who raised Christ from the dead dwells in you, he who raised Christ from the dead will also give life to your mortal bodies through his Spirit that dwells in you”,

**57,7,7** so that the words of the prophets might accord with those of the apostles, and those of the apostles with those of the gospels, and those of the gospels with those of the apostles, and those of the apostles with those of the prophets; for there it says, “[I] raised up a king”, and here, “He who raises Christ from the dead”.

**57,7,8a** And the words “God is in you” [show] how mysteriously and wonderfully the divine Scripture relates all things.

**57,7,8b** For the Godhead, [dwelling] in flesh as in a temple, was seen in advance and announced beforehand to the hope of men through their turning to God.

**57,7,9** For the Son of God, God the Word, dwelling as God as in a holy city and in a holy temple, in the holy incarnation and the coming in the flesh, says with reference to the holy temple, “Pull down this temple, and in three days I will raise it”.

**57,7,10** For God the Word, sent from the Father in the flesh, orders all things in a mystery, and, showing a bond of spiritual love, he draws flesh to himself, having made himself small though he is great God — he, the Word, born from a virgin by the Holy Spirit, being one Son of God, having rendered himself one in flesh and in spirit, as he says, “He who came down is the same who went up, the Son of Man, the one who is in heaven”.

**57,8,1a** What, then, will Noetus say in his mindlessness?

**57,8,1b** Was there flesh in heaven?

**57,8,1c** Clearly there was not.

**57,8,1d** How, then, is he who came down from heaven the same as he who went up?

**57,8,1e** But so that he might signify that the Word who came was not from below but had come down from above, since he became man in the flesh, not from the seed of a man, but having perfected his dispensation from spirit and flesh,

**57,8,2** for this reason, signifying one unity of the Word’s union and of the incarnation, he says that he who came from above goes up above in the perfection of the Godhead.
