**57,8,3** For now indeed he who was never flesh, but spirit, has become flesh from spirit and virgin, the perfect Word offered to the Father, who before this was not flesh in heaven.

**57,8,4** What, then, was the one in heaven but the Word sent from heaven, having the union into one Godhead, united according to the power of the Father, so that he might show that on earth and [in] heaven the same God the Word existed, being unchangeable and unalterable?

**57,8,5** For he was Word, he was ever God, he was spirit, he was power, he who took to himself the name that is common and comprehensible among men, being thus called Son of Man, though he is Son of God;

**57,8,6** and he was named beforehand among the prophets as that which he was not yet in the flesh, because of what was to be perfected in him, as Daniel too said, “I saw one like a son of man, coming on the clouds”.

**57,8,7** Therefore the prophet rightly called the Word, [being] in heaven, [whom] he saw in the Holy Spirit, by this name, Son of Man, contemplating [what was to come] before it came to pass, and calling him Son of Man before he came to be in the flesh.

**57,8,8** Therefore, signifying the first things by the later, the Only-begotten says, “No one has ascended into heaven except he who descended out of heaven, the Son of Man”; not that there was flesh above, but [that] he came down from above, being destined to be called by this name.

**57,9,1a** But what is it that you will say, you there — that “this”, he says, “is our God, and no other shall be reckoned”?

**57,9,1b** And he spoke very well, as the apostle too affirms when he says, “Whose are the fathers, and from whom is Christ according to the flesh, who is God over all”.

**57,9,2** [And] he relates it wonderfully; for this is the one who is God over all; because he himself teaches us, saying, “All things have been delivered to me by my Father”, for this reason he is God over all.

**57,9,3** For so John too bears witness when he says, “What was from the beginning, what we have heard and have beheld with our eyes, and our hands have felt”, and once more he says in the Revelation, “He who is from the beginning and who is coming, the Almighty”.

**57,9,4** He spoke well in every respect; for in saying, “All things have been delivered to me by my Father”, he also implied [the Father] precisely.

**57,9,5a** He is, then, God over all, but he has his own Father, [...] in saying, “I am going away to the Father”.

**57,9,5b** But if he himself were the Father, to what Father, then, is he going away, you mindless man?

**57,10,1a** Or does he not say again, “That they may be one, as I and you are one”?

**57,10,1b** For the Scripture, always guarding against men’s falls towards the extreme, gathers the mind from every side to the middle way of the truth.

**57,10,2** And to those who suppose that the Son is alien to the Father — I mean as Arius supposes, and other heresies — he says, “I and the Father are one”;

**57,10,3** but to those who suppose that the same one is Father and the same one is Son, on account of the words “I and the Father are one”, he says, “Make them that they may be one, even as I and you are one”, that he might put Noetus and his school to shame by bringing forward into the midst the unity of the disciples.

**57,10,4a** For how could Peter and John and the rest be one as though by a blending?

**57,10,4b** But because of being in one unity of Godhead, and in one mind and power, so as to repel every notion that rises up against the truth on either side [...];

**57,10,5a** [...] Philip the holy apostle, saying, “Show us your Father”.

**57,10,5b** And he said, “He who has seen me has seen the Father”, and he never said, “I am the Father”,

**57,10,6a** but in saying “me” he spoke signifying himself; whereas in saying “has seen the Father”, he was not speaking of himself.

**57,10,6b** For “the Father” is one thing, and “me” another, and “I” another, and “me” something different.

**57,10,7** For had he himself been the Father, he would have said, “I am”; but since he is not himself the Father, but the Son, he truly says, “He who has seen me has seen the Father”, to repel the blasphemy of Arius which estranges the Son from the Father.

**57,10,8** Therefore, since all Scripture clearly sets before us the way concerning the truth, let us stop [here], having thrust aside this Noetus and his heresy — the heresy of the Noetians, I mean — together with the other heresies, like the serpent called the ostracites, which cannot turn when it pursues a man, neither to the right nor to the left,

**57,10,9** [and], having turned away from the wickedness both of him and of his school, let us launch our mind, by the power of God, upon the next ones, to describe and refute the strange talk devised in them against the truth.

### Sect 58: Valesians

Against the Valesians, the thirty-eighth, but the fifty-eighth in the series.

**58,1,1** We have often heard about the Valesians, but we have nowhere learned who this Valens was, or where he came from, or what he [was] saying, teaching or uttering.

**58,1,2** And the name, being Arabic, gives us some ground of suspicion to think that he and his heresy are current even to this day, as by way of supposition [...], as I said, that there are some in Bacatha in the territory of Philadelphia beyond the Jordan.

**58,1,3a** And the people of the country choose to call them Gnostics, but they are not of the Gnostics; for their opinions are different from theirs.

**58,1,3b** And what has come to us about them is as follows.

**58,1,4a** Most of them used to assemble in the church until a certain time, until their folly spread abroad and they were cast out of the church.

**58,1,4b** And they are all castrated except a few, and they too hold views about principalities and powers [...] and other things in this way.

**58,1,5** And whenever they take someone on as a disciple, for as long as he has not yet been cut off from his parts, he does not partake of anything that has life; but when they persuade such a man, or castrate him by force, then he partakes of anything whatever, as one who has now ceased from the contest and is no longer in danger of being stirred up through foods to come to the pleasure of desire.

**58,1,6** And they not only fit out their own people in this way, but often they have also subjected strangers passing by and lodging with them to this treatment, as much talk goes about concerning this.

**58,1,7** For they seize such men indoors, and having bound them behind upon benches, they perform by force the handiwork of removing the members.

**58,1,8a** And these are the things that have come to us about them.

**58,1,8b** Having learned in what place these people have their dwelling, and that this name is common in those parts, and since we have not learned another name for the heresy, we have supposed that this is the one.

**58,2,1a** Great is the derangement of such people.

**58,2,1b** For if they wish to fulfil what is in the gospel, “If one of your members offends you, cut it off from you; for it is better for you to enter the kingdom of heaven lame or blind or maimed”, how can anyone be in the kingdom mutilated?

**58,2,2a** For if the kingdom of heaven is what perfects all things, it has nothing imperfect in itself.

**58,2,2b** And since the body has a resurrection, every member will rise, and not one of them will be left behind.

**58,2,3a** And if any member at all fails to be raised, the body as a whole will not rise either.

**58,2,3b** And if only the one member that offends is left behind, then none of the members will rise at all; for all of them have offended us.

**58,2,4a** And who will tear open his heart, which offends in every way?

**58,2,4b** “For from within”, he says, “come forth fornications, adulteries, licentiousness and things like these”.

**58,2,4c** Who, then, will rend his own heart?

**58,2,5a** But if the body is not raised, according to the folly and impiety of some, what difference, then, will the argument laid down by these people make?

**58,2,5b** For if the members, all of them together, do not go into the kingdom of heaven, what need is there any longer for this to be done to one member, when the others do not fulfil it?

**58,2,6a** But if the body is raised — and raised it is — how is it still possible for there to be mutilation in a body in the kingdom of heaven?

**58,2,6b** Or how will the kingdom of heaven not be unseemly if it holds maimed bodies, which are there for the glory of those within it?

**58,2,7** But if the offending member were in fact cut off, then it has been severed and has not sinned; and having been cut off and not having sinned, it ought to rise first of all, since it did not sin.

**58,3,1a** But those who are bold for this rash deed have set themselves aside and estranged themselves from everything.

**58,3,1b** For they are no longer men, because of what has been removed, nor can they be women, since that would be against nature.

**58,3,2a** And the title of the crown of the contest and of this prize has also gone on ahead.

**58,3,2b** For these people will not prove to belong to any of the three orders of eunuchs spoken of by the Lord.

**58,3,2c** “For there are”, he says, “eunuchs who were born from their mother’s womb”.

**58,3,3** For those are not to blame for their eunuchhood, having neither sin because they were born so, nor again praise, since they are unable to do [any such thing] — I mean the acts of love — because the organs for begetting children, created by God, have not grown in them.

**58,3,4** But neither do they have the kingdom of heaven as the reward of eunuchhood, because they have not been initiated into the contest.

**58,3,5a** And even if such men have been initiated into desires, since they are unable to do what ought not to be done, they have no reward for not doing it.

**58,3,5b** For it is not because they do not wish it that the deed has not been done by them, but because they are unable.

**58,3,6** And this is the condition of the first eunuchhood spoken of by the Lord, that which is born in eunuchhood through their mothers, to which these people cannot belong, because theirs is the work of hands.

**58,4,1a** “And there are eunuchs who were made eunuchs by men”, says the Saviour, and neither are the people mentioned above of these.

**58,4,1b** For these come to be (those made eunuchs by men) for the sake of service, either royal or in the households of rulers.

**58,4,2** For some kings of the barbarians, or tyrants too, wishing it out of jealousy and suspicion towards their own wives, take boys while still quite young and make them eunuchs, so that when they have grown up they may be entrusted, as I said, with their own wives.

**58,4,3a** And so the reason for these people arose in the world.

**58,4,3b** From this, I think, the name of eunuch also perhaps [came about], from their being able to be of good mind once the members are removed, and from their not being worn down by the union of bodies, as men deprived of the organs.

**58,4,4** This, then, is a different order, taken from childhood and made eunuchs by men, not for the sake of the kingdom of heaven.

**58,4,5a** “And there are”, he says, “eunuchs who have made themselves eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom of heaven”.

**58,4,5b** Who, then, would these be but the noble apostles and, after them, the virgins and monks?

**58,4,6** John and James, the sons of Zebedee, remained in virginity, neither cutting off their members with their own hands nor being joined in marriage, but contending in their own heart and wonderfully carrying off the glory of the crown of this contest.

**58,4,7** And after them, all those, numbering myriads, who, living as monks in the world and from monasteries and houses of virgins, have carried off this glory of the contest, who have not had intercourse with the bodies of women, but have striven in a most perfect contest;

**58,4,8a** as Elijah too is found in the Old Testament, and Paul, saying, “I say to the unmarried that it is well for them to stay as they are, just as I am; but if they do not exercise self-control, let them marry”.

**58,4,8b** How, then, did he himself remain?

**58,4,9a** For if he had been castrated, and they remained in his way, being made like him so as to fulfil the words “even as I”, how could anyone who had been castrated marry, if he no longer exercised self-control, according to the words “let them marry and not burn”?

**58,4,9b** You see that he is speaking of self-control and not of the removal of members.

**58,4,10** But if they say that they have made themselves eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom of heaven, how will they estrange themselves from the saying that was spoken, “There are some who were made eunuchs by men”?

**58,4,11** For if someone castrates himself by his own hand, he is a man, and it is his hands that have wrought this lawless deed; but if he could not manage it on himself and was castrated by others instead, it will no longer be for the sake of the kingdom of heaven, because he has been made a eunuch by men, whether through himself or through those who did it.

**58,4,12** And he will fall away both from the crown and from the prize, no longer having any credit for abstaining from such use, because he is unable to use it, the members serving such a purpose having been removed.

**58,4,13a** And he will be judged one and the same, both as one who mutilates his own member and as one who cuts down another man’s vineyard.

**58,4,13b** For such a man has not lived according to God, but has set himself treacherously against the Lord God the Creator.

**58,4,14a** And such a man will be carried away by a greater desire.

**58,4,14b** For the eunuch is spoken of by the Wise Man as not abstaining from desire, but [...] as unable to fulfil the desire, as he says, “A eunuch’s longing to rob a young girl of her virginity”.

**58,4,15** And from every side [...] oh, the great vanity contrived in the world in so many ways!

**58,4,16a** And these are the things we have learned about them.

**58,4,16b** Therefore, having spoken briefly about them, and having supposed, as I said, that these are the people mentioned above,

**58,4,17** let us take leave of them and mock [them], as one mocks a scorpion with two stings that has opposed with its horns and claws the lives of the fathers from of old, and with its sting resists the pattern of the holy church of God; and having trodden these people down with a firmly planted sandal — that is, through the words of the gospel — and going on, according to our custom, to those that follow, let us here leave off from their stupidity.

### Sect 59: Cathari

Against the Cathari, who are unclean, the thirty-ninth, but the fifty-ninth in the series.

**59,1,1a** Next after these there sprang up certain people called Cathari, from one Novatus, as much talk has it.

**59,1,1b** This Novatus lived at Rome, at the time of the persecution which took place before Maximian; it was then, I think, perhaps that of Decius, or of Aurelian.

**59,1,2** On the pretext of those who had lapsed in the persecution, lifted up by pride, he, together with those who followed him, refused to have communion with those who repented after the persecution, and turned to a heresy of this kind, saying that there is no salvation [for them], but one repentance, and that after the washing no one who has lapsed can any longer obtain mercy.

**59,1,3** We ourselves also affirm that repentance is one, and that it is through the washing of regeneration that this salvation is accomplished, but we do not do away with the love of God for mankind,

**59,1,4** knowing the preaching of the truth and the mercy of the Master, and the pardonableness of our nature, the soul’s being easily tossed about, the weakness of the flesh, the abundant welling-up of the senses of all men into transgressions, because “no one is sinless and clean from defilement, not even if his life upon the earth be but one day”.

**59,1,5a** And the perfect repentance is found in the washing; but if anyone has fallen away, the holy church of God does not destroy him.

**59,1,5b** For it grants a way back as well, and, after repentance, contrition.

**59,1,6** For God said to Cain, “You have sinned, be still”, and to the paralytic, “Behold, you have been made well; sin no more”; and the Lord calls Peter back after his denial, and in place of the three denials he calls him three times to confession: “Do you love me, Peter? Do you love me, Peter? Do you love me, Peter?”, and he says, “Feed my little sheep”.

**59,2,1** But they are misled by the saying of the apostle which runs, “For those who have once been enlightened and have tasted God’s good word and the powers of the age to come, and have fallen away — it is impossible to renew them again to repentance, as they crucify the Son of God again for themselves and hold him up to contempt.

**59,2,2** For the earth which drinks the rain that often comes upon it, and brings forth herbage fit for those for whose sake it is also tilled, receives blessing; but if it bears thorns and thistles, it is rejected and near to a curse, and its end is for burning”.

**59,2,3a** And indeed it is in truth impossible to renew those who have once been renewed and have fallen away.

**59,2,3b** For Christ will not be born a second time to be crucified on our behalf, nor can anyone crucify afresh the Son of God, who is crucified no more, nor can anyone receive a second washing; for there is one baptism and one renewal.

**59,2,4** But at once the holy apostle adds the cure of these things, healing the church and caring for its members, and says, “But we are persuaded of better things concerning you, beloved, things that belong to salvation, though we speak thus. For God is not unjust so as to forget your good work”.

**59,2,5** And you see how he declared outright that the renewal cannot take place a second time, but did not cut off from salvation those who still repent; rather, he pointed to the things that belong to salvation, and to the Lord as God the helper, for the sake of their good works, of those who even after transgressions repent most perfectly, turn back and feel contrition.

**59,2,6** So the holy Word and the holy church of God always accept repentance, not becoming watered down nor making slack those who are completing the race, but not cutting short God’s grace and love for mankind, and knowing the manner of each case.

**59,2,7** For just as one who has lost virginity cannot [re]gain it bodily, since nature does not allow it, so too one who after the washing has fallen into some great transgressions;

**59,2,8a** and just as one who has fallen away from virginity holds continence as a second honour, so too one who after the washing has come into a greater transgression has a second cure, [contrition], not so excellent as the first.

**59,2,8b** Yet the second, which he has acquired, he holds as not cast out from life.

**59,2,8c** The divine Word, then, does not set aside the reward of those who labour in repentance.

**59,3,1a** But then the same people, leaping on further again from this, invented certain other things as well.

**59,3,1b** For they too say that they hold the same faith which we also possess; but they will not have communion with the twice-married.

**59,3,1c** For if anyone after baptism should be joined to a second wife, he will no longer be received among them.

**59,3,2a** And the whole thing is foolish.

**59,3,2b** For it is as if someone should see a man swimming in water and, not knowing the skill of swimming across, should give himself up to the waters and go under, not being experienced nor clearly knowing the art of those who procure safety for themselves by hands and feet, but simply supposing that the water carries a man across without his own hands;

**59,3,3** or as if someone should hear that the governor punishes those who do [works] of wickedness, even down to the least, and should suppose that the penalty is carried out equally against all, the same for murder as for one who slanders his neighbour or [...] has quarrelled;

**59,3,4** or as if someone who is altogether a private person should see one who has received a magistrate’s authority draw the sword against the guilty, or against sorcerers and blasphemers or the unhallowed, and should suppose that everyone has authority to exact the penalty for such a cause, and, having seen men punished, should himself imitate the same and wish to kill some people, supposedly to judge the wrongdoers, but being caught he will himself pay the penalty,

**59,3,5** since he had not received authority over such matters from the emperor, and because he thought that the same sentence went forth against all from the justice of the laws, having made himself liable to destruction through the wrong of his own inexperience and lack of understanding —

**59,3,6** so too these people, having mixed up everything together, have fallen away from everything, and, not understanding the exactness of God’s teaching, they have slipped and turned aside on to another path, not knowing that this is not the tradition and the sequence of the divine Scripture.

**59,4,1a** For the things handed down for the priesthood, because of the supreme excellence of the sacred ministry, they supposed applied equally to all.

**59,4,1b** For having heard that “a bishop must be beyond reproach, husband of a single wife, self-controlled”, and “likewise also the deacon”, and the presbyter, they did not understand the limit set for the orders.

**59,4,2** For indeed the holy preaching of God, since the sojourn of Christ, does not admit to the priesthood even those who, after their wife has died, have passed from a first marriage and been joined in a second, because of the surpassing honour of the priesthood; and the holy church of God guards these things securely and with exactness.

**59,4,3** But it does not even accept one who still [co]habits and begets children, though he is the husband of one wife; but it accepts as deacon and presbyter and bishop and subdeacon one who has practised continence from his one wife, or has been widowed, especially where the ecclesiastical canons are strict.

**59,4,4a** But you will surely tell me that in some places presbyters and deacons and subdeacons still beget children.

**59,4,4b** This, however, does not come about because of the canon, but because of men’s thinking, which has grown slack in the course of time, and because a ministry for the multitude is not to be found otherwise.

**59,4,5** For the church, ever [over]seeing what is most fitting, well ordered by the Holy Spirit, has recognised that it must strive for the services to God to be performed “without distraction”, and for the spiritual duties to be fulfilled with the most well-disposed conscience in every respect.

**59,4,6** And I say that it is fitting, because of sudden services and needs, that the presbyter and deacon and bishop should be free for God.

**59,4,7a** For if the holy apostle commands even those of the laity, saying that they should “devote themselves for a time to prayer”, how much more does he command the same of the priest?

**59,4,7b** And by being undistracted I mean being free for the priesthood that is carried out according to God in the spiritual duties.

**59,4,8** But [among] the laity it is permitted to bear with [this] because of weakness, and for [those] who could not stand firm with their first wife to be joined to a second after the death of the first.

**59,4,9** And the man who has had one wife is held in greater praise and honour by all members of the church; but the man who was unable to make do with his one wife once she had died, [or] who, for some reason — fornication or adultery or another cause — when a separation has taken place, has been joined to a second wife, or a woman to a second husband, the divine Word does not blame, nor does it banish him from the church and from life, but bears with him because of his weakness;

**59,4,10** not so that he may have two wives at the same time while one is still living, but [so that], if it should so happen, having been parted from one he may lawfully be joined to a second — the holy Word and the holy church of God have mercy on such a man, especially if he is otherwise devout and lives according to the law of God.

**59,4,11** For the apostle would not have said to the widows, “Let them marry, bear children, rule their households”, nor again would he have said, of the man who was living with his father’s wife and had been handed over “to Satan for the destruction of the flesh, so that the spirit may be saved on the day of the Lord” — he would not have said again, “Confirm your love for him, so that such a man may not be overwhelmed by too much grief”.

**59,4,12a** For he said again, “To whom you forgive anything, I forgive also; for if I have forgiven anything, I have forgiven it for your sakes in the presence of the Lord, lest we be taken advantage of by Satan. For we are not ignorant of his designs”.

**59,4,12b** And see how he grants repentance even after a transgression.

**59,5,1** Again, the Lord says, “Pardon one another’s transgressions, that your Father who is in heaven may also forgive you”.

**59,5,2** And in another place too he speaks thus, “And I shall mourn many of those among you who have fallen and have not repented”, as showing that if they have fallen and have repented, these become acceptable and will not be cast out, the Lord knowing what he will do with each one.

**59,5,3a** And that the canon of the truth has such a character is clear to everyone.

**59,5,3b** For after the first repentance, that through the washing of regeneration, in which repentance every man was renewed, there is no second of the same kind.

**59,5,4a** For baptisms are not two but one; nor was Christ crucified two times, but a single time; nor did he twice die for us and rise again.

**59,5,4b** And for this reason one must make oneself secure, lest by falling away we lose the crown of the renewal.

**59,5,5a** But if anyone falls and “is overtaken in some transgression”, as the apostle says, “you who are spiritual, restore such a one in a spirit of meekness, looking to yourself, lest you too be tempted”.

**59,5,5b** If, then, anyone is overtaken [in] a transgression, of whatever kind it may be, let him repent.

**59,5,6a** For God accepts repentance even after baptism, if anyone falls away.

**59,5,6b** But how he deals with such a one, he alone knows; “for his judgments are unsearchable and his ways beyond telling”.

**59,5,7a** And before the coming one must not judge, “until the Lord comes, who will also reveal the hidden things of the heart; and then each one’s praise will become manifest”.

**59,5,7b** “For the day will declare it, because it is revealed in fire”.

**59,6,1** So we neither promise complete freedom to those who have fallen after baptism, nor do we despair of their life; for God “is merciful and compassionate”, and “he has given a way back to those who repent”.

**59,6,2a** And the first point is clear, while as regards the second, we are aware that God is merciful provided that with all our soul we repent of our transgressions.

**59,6,2b** For he holds in his hand life and salvation and love for mankind.

**59,6,2c** And what he does is known only to himself, except that there is no loss from repentance, nor is the one who repents of all his faults despaired of.

**59,6,3a** How much more the man who has lawfully been joined to a second wife?

**59,6,3b** And the first wife is appointed by God, but the second comes from the weakness of men; and if he should be joined to yet another, the weakness is still always borne with.

**59,6,4a** For he says, “A wife is bound by law for as long as her husband lives; but if the husband dies, she is free to be married to whom she wishes, [only in the Lord]”.

**59,6,4b** Having declared, after a husband’s death, freedom from sin without any dispute, by the addition he signifies [...] with the words “in the Lord”;

**59,6,5** that the woman who after her husband’s death is joined to another husband, or the man who after his wife’s death is joined to a second wife, is not alien to the Lord — “only in the Lord”, in the apostle’s words.

**59,6,6** And he says [indeed], “But she is more blessed if she remains so”, [...] and “in the Lord” means not in fornication, not in adultery, not in clandestine marriage, but in good order, in openness, in honourable marriage, persevering in the faith, in the commandments, in good deeds, in piety, in fasts, in good order, in almsgiving, in zeal, in well-doing.

**59,6,7** For these things, when present and abiding, render people neither idle nor unfruitful for the coming of the Lord.

**59,6,8** And to the priesthood belong the first rank and exactness about everything, but to the laity moderation and pardon, so that all may be taught and receive mercy.

**59,6,9a** For the Master is merciful and able to save all by their own good order and by a true faith in the purity of the preaching.

**59,6,9b** For he alone is pure.

**59,6,10** But these people, calling themselves Cathari, make themselves unclean by this very claim; for everyone who has declared himself pure has utterly condemned himself as unclean.

**59,7,1a** Great is the folly of such people, who think that because of one thing, even if it were true, they may bring judgment in this way upon the whole people.

**59,7,1b** They ought to have known that it is not about this matter alone that every soul is open to accusation,

**59,7,2** or that it [is possible] to become virtuous through this alone, but also by not reviling, by not swearing an oath either in truth or in falsehood, but saying “Yes, yes” and “No, no”, by not acting deceitfully, not slandering, not stealing, not engaging in trade.

**59,7,3** For from all these things the filth of sins is gathered upon us; “for as a peg”, he says, “will be crushed between two stones, so sin between buyer and seller”.

**59,7,4** And that out of the whole multitude [some] are drunkards and traders and covetous and usurers [...], and that surely [they themselves] too have such faults and things like them, [and] that falsehoods attend each of them.

**59,7,5a** How, then, can they call themselves Cathari, [as though] through this one matter the whole practice of godliness were made secure against all faults?

**59,7,5b** But these people have not known the exactness of the preaching, nor to whom it assigned this strictness about second marriage.

**59,7,6** But even those who have lapsed in the persecution, if they too show a perfect repentance, having sat down in sackcloth and ashes and wept before the Lord — the Benefactor is able to have mercy on them as well; for nothing bad comes of repentance.

**59,7,7** So the Lord and his church receive those who repent, as Manasseh the son of Hezekiah turned back and was received by the Lord, and the holy Peter, who denied for an hour, the very chief of the apostles,

**59,7,8** who has truly become for us a firm rock founding the faith of the Lord, on which the church is built in every way;

**59,7,9a** first, in that he confessed “Christ, the Son of the living God”, and heard, “On this rock of sure faith I will build my church”, since he clearly confessed him as true Son.

**59,7,9b** For when he said “Son of the living God”, adding the words “the living”, he showed that he is the true-born Son, as I have said about this in almost every heresy.

**59,8,1** But concerning the Holy Spirit too the same man makes us secure, saying to Ananias and those with him, “Why is it that Satan has tempted you to deceive the Holy Spirit? You have not lied to man, but to God”, because the Spirit is from God and not alien to God.

**59,8,2** But here too he has become a firm rock of the building and the foundation of the house of God, in that, having denied and turned back again and been found by the Lord, he was counted worthy to hear again, “Feed my lambs, and feed my sheep”.

**59,8,3** For Christ, in saying these things, brought us to the conversion of repentance, so that in him the well-founded faith might be built up again, the faith that does not despair of the life of those men who still breathe in this life and repent in truth and correct their own faults in this age.

**59,9,1** As in the Song of Songs too the bride herself spoke to the bridegroom, saying, “My beloved answers and says to me, Arise, come, my companion, my fair one, my dove, for the winter has passed”: the hateful and gloomy things of the clouded sky and the most fearful [...], so to speak, [...] have passed;

**59,9,2a** “the rain has gone, it has gone its way.

**59,9,2b** The flowers are seen in our land, the season of pruning is here, the turtle-dove’s voice is heard in our land. The fig tree has brought out its early figs, our vines are in flower, they have yielded their scent”;

**59,9,3** to show that all things past have gone by, now that spring is just blossoming, the sea grown calm, the fear of the rain gone by, and the old [...] of the vine pruned away, and the grass is no longer only growing grass,

**59,9,4** but is also putting forth flowers; and the preaching of the voice crying in the desert — that is, in our land; and the fig tree that was once cursed putting forth its green figs, the fruits of repentance already appearing on the branches and trees; and the vines already blossoming with the fragrant preaching of the faith of the gospel.

**59,9,5** And since he at once called such a one and said, “Arise, come”, [...] rise up from the deadness of sins and come in righteousness, rise up from transgression and come in boldness, arise from sins and come in repentance, arise from paralysis and come in perfection, arise from mutilation and come in health, rise from unbelief and come in faith, rise from perdition and come in being found.

**59,9,6** And since the divine oracle knew that after the first repentance and after the first calling and healing, so to speak, many transgressions befall humankind, it says again, “Arise, come, my companion, my fair one, my dove, and come, you my dove”.

**59,9,7a** He calls her a second time and not once only.

**59,9,7b** But the second is not equal to the first.

**59,9,7c** For in the former call he speaks thus, “Arise, come, my companion, my fair one, my dove”.

**59,9,7d** The first time, “Arise, come”, and he did not say, “Come, you”.

**59,9,8** And in the second instance he supplies the article, so as to show a call that is not a second one, altered after the first, but one within the first, stretching out again, after transgressions, a favouring and heavenly hand of love for mankind.

**59,9,9** “And come”, he says, “[you] my dove, in the shelter of the rock, close by the outer wall”: “in the shelter of the rock”, [that is], in the love of Christ for mankind and in the mercy of the Lord; for the rock’s shelter is this — a shelter of hope and faith and truth.

**59,9,10** “Close by the outer wall”: that is, before the gate is shut, before the king, having gone within the walls, admits no more — that is, after the departure from here and death — when it is no longer “close by the outer wall”, but the gates have been shut and there is no longer any setting things right.

**59,10,1** For in the age to come, after a man’s departure from here, there is no gain from fasting, no call to repentance, no bestowing of alms; but neither are there deeds that bring accusation, no war, no adultery, no wantonness — nor yet righteousness and repentance.

**59,10,2** For just as after the reaping of the ear the grain cannot grow plump, nor become blighted by the wind, [...] there is no other good condition whatever.

**59,10,3a** But do not speak to me of the things that ruin a storehouse, that is, both worms and moths; for these are said with reference to what happens in the world, whereas what is portrayed, and shut in for us within gates and made safe in a storehouse, is a model and outline of faith, “where neither thieves break in nor moths destroy”, as the divine word has it.

**59,10,3b** [...] Nor indeed is there any gain of godliness, nor [a call] to repentance, as I said, after death.

**59,10,4** For neither does Lazarus go off to the rich man there, nor the rich man to Lazarus, nor does Abraham, putting the poor man to trouble, send him who was afterwards to be rich, nor does the rich man, now become poor, receive [a share of] what he asks, although he besought the merciful Abraham with supplication.

**59,10,5** For the storehouses have been sealed, and the time has been fulfilled, and the contest has been finished, and the arena has been emptied, and the crowns have been given, and those who contended have gone to their rest, and those who did not arrive in time have let it go, and those who did not contend no longer have the means, and those beaten in the arena have been thrown out, and all things have plainly been brought to their end after the departure from here.

**59,10,6** But while all are still in the world, even after a fall there is a rising up, there is still hope, still healing, still confession, even if not in the most perfect manner, [...] yet at all events the salvation of the others [too] is not given up.

**59,11,1a** But every heresy, having run aground away from the truth, is blind in darkness and dim of sight, conceiving some things in place of others.

**59,11,1b** For these people resemble certain unskilled folk who do not know the things [concerning] the nature and use of each article and the adornment of each limb, and, so to speak (what I am offering to say is something laughable, [but] it is like their folly), are such as to put the shoes on the head, the crown on the feet and a golden collar round the belly;

**59,11,2** and the other footwear, so to speak, the kind made of cloth, which among some people is called socks or breeches, they put on their hands, and rings on their feet.

**59,11,3a** For in this way the ordering of those who have possessed themselves of ignorance is found to be unsteady and disorderly.

**59,11,3b** For the things strictly secured in the case of the priesthood concerning a second marriage and other matters these people have deemed [...] for the whole people, and the things proclaimed by God with exceeding severity, so that some might not grow careless and fall away, they themselves have ascribed to God to the point of inhumanity.

**59,11,4** And as if someone, having got hold of the sleeve of a garment, should cover himself only as far as the elbow from what is called the wrist of the hand, and should hold the sleeve up before his eyes every hour and jeer at the others, forgetting that his whole body is naked,

**59,11,5a** so too are these people, who make a boast of not admitting those twice married, in respect of all the commandments.

**59,11,5b** Those like this one, and surpassingly finer still in the keeping, yet deadly in not being kept, [...] — they forbid the one, and have abandoned the others.

**59,11,6** And, having forgotten, they go about with the whole body naked, having let all the precepts lie idle and holding the one commandment without purity.

**59,12,1a** And oh, the great vain-mindedness of mankind!

**59,12,1b** Yet every pretext, even if it was a slight one, has drawn each heresy away from the truth and brought it down into a great torrent of evils.

**59,12,2** For just as, [if] someone, finding a gap in a fence beside a highway, should think to walk through it, and leaving the road should turn [thither], thinking that the point from which he might bend round and regain the road was very near, but should not know that there is a wall of great height, built over a long stretch,

**59,12,3** and should run along without finding through which [opening] he may make his way out, pressing on step by step still further, beyond a single milestone or mile, without finding the road, and so should turn to yet greater distances, and, wearying himself in this way and at a loss how he might bring himself back to his journey, perhaps he could not find it at all, unless he were to turn back by the way through which he had also come in —

**59,12,4** so too each heresy, thinking to discover some short cut, has run aground through the long way round, and the entanglement of folly and ignorance has proved for it an unbreakable wall;

**59,12,5** and such a heresy would not succeed in reaching the true road, unless each were to turn back to the original pattern of the road, that is, the royal road;

**59,12,6** just as the law too cried out in plain words, when holy Moses said to Edom’s king, “This is what your brother Israel says: I will pass along your borders into the land that the Lord swore to give to our fathers, a land that flows with milk and honey, the country of the Amorites and Perizzites and Girgashites and Jebusites and Hivites and Canaanites and Hittites.

**59,12,7** We will not turn aside to the right or to the left; we will drink water for silver and eat food for silver; we will not turn aside this way or that; we will walk by the royal road.”

**59,12,8a** For there is a royal road, and this is God’s church and the journey of the truth.

**59,12,8b** But each of these, having forsaken the royal road and turned aside to the right or to the left, giving itself over further and further from then on, will be drawn away into error, and with each heresy the pathlessness of error no longer has any measure.

**59,13,1** Come then, servants of God and sons of the holy church of God, who know the sure rule and walk the road of the truth, let us not be drawn aside by voices and led by the voice of each counterfeit pursuit.

**59,13,2** For their roads are slippery, and the path of their counterfeit thinking is steep; they boast loudly, and do not know even the small things; they promise freedom, being themselves slaves of sin; they vaunt the greater things, and have not laid hold of the small ones at all.

**59,13,3** But concerning these too, the so-called Cathari — unclean, rather, if the truth must be told — I consider it sufficient that enough has been said up to this point.

**59,13,4** And, flinging this one away like a basilisk’s face — a creature that in the outward show of its name possesses a most exalted title, yet by the encounter causes death — let us, having struck it by the might of the cross’s wood, press on once more to the others, invoking that very supplication to God,

**59,13,5** that he may travel with us, remain beside us, be present with us, labour with us, keep us safe, teach us, and deem us worthy of speaking the truth, lest we ourselves, by saying anything falsely, incur the same things as the heresies that did not teach the world the truth.

**59,13,6** Further, those in Africa and Byzacena too, called Donatians after a certain Donatus, who hold views like these, rebels themselves as well, supposedly because they will not hold communion with those who fell away in the persecution, will be overthrown by the same arguments, just as the Novatians too, who are yoked with them under an alien yoke, otherwise called Cathari.

**59,13,7** Therefore we have had no need to work out anything further about them, but we have joined them together with those who resemble them.

**59,13,8a** These, however, have fallen away once more, still more grievously and worse.

**59,13,8b** For they hold the faith in accordance with Arius; and they will be overthrown in the same way concerning the faith on which they think wrongly, as Arius too was overthrown by words of truth, since he thought the same as they, and they as he.

**59,13,9** And passing by this one too, we will walk on to those that follow, having trampled down, as it were, dreadful reptiles in the Lord.

### Sect 60: Angelics

Against the Angelics, the fortieth, but the sixtieth in the series.

**60,1,1a** We have heard of a heresy of Angelics [...], and only the name has sounded in our ears, but we do not know at all clearly what this heresy is, because perhaps, having arisen at some time or other, it later ceased and was brought to an end completely.

**60,1,1b** But for what reason it received its name we do not know.

**60,1,2** For either it is because some say that the world came into being through angels (and whether this heresy, saying this, is called by this name, I cannot say), or because they pride themselves on being in the rank of angels and on leading a most exalted life (nor do I affirm this either), or else they took the name from some place, since there is a district called Angeline lying beyond the country of Mesopotamia.

**60,2,1a** But now that you, the hearer, have been reminded, you will not fall into suspicions so as to condemn [us].

**60,2,1b** For we promised to make plain the roots and the nourishment of some, or something of what is done among them, but of others to make mention only by name;

**60,2,2** and, as the divine power has provided and given help, having gone through them all as far as this heresy, we have left none unexplained except this one alone.

**60,2,3** But perhaps because, after being puffed up with conceit for a short time, it later ceased, we did not gain any very clear grasp of it.

**60,2,4** Having briefly mentioned its name alone, then, and treating its name like an untimely birth, we shall pass this place by,

**60,2,5** looking now to make our onset against the others, and asking the Master of all to appear at our side and, having displayed the exact particulars of what is done among them, to make everything known with precision to our small mind,

**60,2,6** so that we may bring about, for ourselves and for our neighbours, the correction of fleeing from what is base, and may procure for ourselves firmness in God in what is good and most true.

### Sect 61: Apostolics

Against the Apostolics, the forty-first, but the sixty-first in the series.

**61,1,1** After these, others named themselves Apostolics, and they also like to call themselves Apotactics; for among them the practice of owning nothing is kept.

**61,1,2** These people as well are a fragment broken off from Tatian’s teachings, from the Encratites and Tatianists and Cathari, who do not accept marriage at all; and among them the mysteries too have been altered.

**61,1,3** They pride themselves, to be sure, on owning nothing, but these people make schisms to no purpose and harm the holy church of God, having fallen away from God’s love for mankind through their self-chosen worship.

**61,1,4** For neither does readmission any longer take place [among] them for anyone who has lapsed, and they hold views like those stated above concerning marriage and the other matters.

**61,1,5** And the Cathari use only the recognised Scriptures, whereas these lean for the most part on what are called the Acts of Andrew and of Thomas, being wholly foreign to the canon of the church.

**61,1,6a** For if marriage is abominable, then [those] born through marriage are all unclean.

**61,1,6b** And if the holy church of God belongs solely to those who have bidden farewell to marriage, then marriage is no longer from God.

**61,1,7** And if it is not, then every matter of begetting children is foreign to God; and if the matter of begetting children is foreign to God, then they themselves too, born of such, are foreign to God.

**61,1,8a** Where, then, is the saying, “What God has joined together, let not man put asunder”?

**61,1,8b** For to separate under compulsion belongs to man, but continence practised by free choice he showed to be the work not of man but of God.

**61,1,9** And natural necessity often incurs blame, because what is compulsory is not carried out with blessing, but has stepped outside the rule; for godliness is not a matter of compulsion, but righteousness comes from free choice.

**61,1,10** But the things that are by nature necessary for godliness [...] are evident, which have been measured out to nature, such as not to commit fornication, not to commit adultery, not to be licentious, not to take a second partner in marriage at the same time, not to rob, not to do wrong, not to get drunk, not to be gluttonous, not to worship idols, not to murder, not to practise sorcery, not to curse, not to revile, not to swear, to be indignant and quickly appeased, to be angry and not sin, not to let the sun go down upon one’s anger;

**61,1,11** but being joined in marriage with good order [...] nature will make plain, created by God and permitted; and all the other things that are of this kind hold the measure of permission in the other sense.

**61,2,1** But, as we said beforehand concerning them, these people are found in a small area, around Phrygia and Cilicia and Pamphylia.

**61,2,2a** What then?

**61,2,2b** Is the church that reaches from the ends of the earth to the ends of the earth cut off?

**61,2,2c** And will it no longer [stand that] “their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words to the ends of the world”?

**61,2,2d** Nor will the word spoken by the Saviour stand any longer, “You shall be my witnesses to the end of the earth”?

**61,2,3a** For if marriage is not held in dignity, nor marriage in keeping with God, nor marriage a thing possessed of life, [...] let them come into being apart from marriage; but if it is from marriage that they are born, then they are profane on account of marriage.

**61,2,3b** But if they alone are not profane,

**61,2,4** although they are indeed from marriage, then marriage is not profane, since without it no one will ever be born in the world.

**61,2,5** And great is the error of mankind, which through variety and through many pretexts has wrought what is base in human nature and led each one astray from the truth by some pretext.

**61,3,1a** And so the church does have renunciation, but it does not abhor marriage; and it has poverty, but it does not lift itself up against those who hold possessions in righteousness and have them from their own parents.

**61,3,1b** This may suffice both for themselves and for those in need.

**61,3,2** And many abstain from foods, but they are not puffed up against those who do not abstain; “for let him who eats not despise him who does not eat, and let him who does not eat not judge him who eats; for he eats and drinks to the Lord, and to the Lord he does not eat and does not drink”.

**61,3,3** And you see one concord and one hope and one faith in the church, bestowed on each according to his own strength and according to his own contest of toil.

**61,3,4a** And the holy church of God is like a ship.

**61,3,4b** Now a ship is not built from a single timber, but from different ones: it has its keel from one timber, yet not in a single piece, while its anchors come [from] another; and the wales and planks and ribs, the uprights, and the parts of the stern and of the sides and of the thwarts, of the sail and of the steering-oars, of the cleats and of the neck-pieces, of the tillers and of all the rest, have their assembly from different timbers.

**61,3,5** But each of these heresies, being a sort of dugout hewn from a single timber, does not display the character of the church; whereas the holy church of God has marriage held in honour and esteems it, since “marriage is honourable and the bed undefiled”,

**61,3,6** [and] it has most admirable continence and praises it, since it contends in a contest and has scorned this life, as being yet more able; and it has virginity and glorifies it above measure, as being something full of virtue and adorned with the lightest of wings; it has those who have renounced the world and are not puffed up against those who are still in the world,

**61,3,7** but rejoice over such people as being most devout, just as the apostles too, while being without possessions themselves, [...] and the Saviour himself, being Master of all,

**61,3,8** having come in the flesh, acquired nothing from the earth, yet did not cast off the women who ministered to him and to his disciples; for it says, “who followed him from Galilee, ministering to him and to those with him from their own possessions”.

**61,4,1** Otherwise how will the saying be fulfilled, “Come to my right hand, you who are blessed, for whom my heavenly Father laid up the kingdom before the world was founded; for I hungered and you gave me food, I thirsted and you gave me drink, I was naked and you clothed me”?

**61,4,2** And from where would these people have had the means to do these things, if not from the righteous labours [which] they carried out and from their own possessions held in righteousness?

**61,4,3** And if they themselves, [those] who have renounced and live the apostolic life, were mingled with the others, their character would not be alien, nor estranged from the ordinance of God;

**61,4,4** and if they had given up wives on account of continence, their resolve would have been worthy of praise, so long as they did not abhor marriage, but treated those still [living] in marriage as partners, knowing the measure and the rank of each.

**61,4,5** For the ship of God takes every passenger, except a robber; for when it recognises a robber and pirate, it does not take him on board, nor a runaway who has thrown off the rule of his own masters.

**61,4,6** So too the holy church of God does not receive fornication, does not receive adultery, does not receive denial of God, does not receive those who set at naught the authority of the ordinance of God and of his apostles,

**61,4,7** but it receives one engaged in a great business, another skilled in seamanship, another a helmsman and [...] expert, one in the bow, another in the stern, possessing the greatest experience in keeping watch, another too who is of some measure of substance and cargo, and another too only so far as to make the crossing and not be sunk in the sea.

**61,4,8a** And it is not at all the case that it does not grant salvation to the one who has not acquired so much; rather it knows how to save all, but each according to his own business.

**61,4,8b** And how is it that those of the household of Caesar send greetings in the apostle?

**61,4,9** And how is it that the apostle has the saying, “If he thinks he is behaving unbecomingly towards his own virgin, and he ought to act thus, let him marry; he does not sin”?

**61,4,10a** But apart from the washing he cannot fulfil the not sinning.

**61,4,10b** For if “all have sinned and lack the glory of God, being justified as a gift by grace”, it is plain [that] it is through the washing of regeneration.

**61,4,10c** For the washing adorned the soul and the body, the soul having washed away every sin through repentance.

**61,4,11** So then the gift of baptism has been spread over the woman who is a virgin as well, and it hastens to set its seal on the woman who is not a virgin, because of her sin.

**61,5,1** But let not some people, in their shallowness, suppose again, if we say that the apostle gave directions about a virgin’s marrying, that it was from a wish to drag down from her own course the woman who has once vowed virginity to God that the apostle gave these directions.

**61,5,2** For the passage is not about these, but about virgins past their prime, who remained so through their prime not for the sake of virginity, but because there was a lack of men for marriage.

**61,5,3** For the apostles, setting out from among the Jews and having lived according to the law, made the beginning of the preaching while still held by the ordinances that are in the law, not according to some justification of the flesh, but according to the law’s fitting safeguard and exactness.

**61,5,4a** For the law admirably proclaimed that the Israelites should not give their daughters to foreigners, lest they lead them astray to the idols.

**61,5,4b** Each of those who believed at that time, then, was charged no longer to give his own virgin in marriage to Jews, but to Christians of the same belief and the same mind.

**61,5,5a** But since the preaching was new, there was not yet a multitude of Christians and of Christian teaching in place after place.

**61,5,5b** So those who at that time had virgin daughters, not having the means to give them in marriage to Christians, kept their own virgins for a long time, and these, passing their prime, fell into fornication through natural necessity.

**61,5,6** The apostle, at any rate, seeing the harm [that] was coming about through the strictness, gave leave, saying, “But if anyone wishes [to give in marriage] his own virgin”; and he did not say “his own virginity”, for he was not speaking about one’s own body,

**61,5,7** but about the father who keeps a virgin; but if by “his own virgin” he also means one’s own body, nothing will stand in the way.

**61,5,8a** He says, then, “[He who] stands firm in his own mind and ought to do this, let him marry,” he says, “he does not sin.

**61,5,8b** Let him marry her off to whomever he may find; he does not sin.”

**61,5,8c** And for this reason he says, “You are bound to a wife: do not seek release; you are released from a wife: do not seek a wife.”

**61,5,8d** He who says, “I wish all to be as I myself am”, said also, “If they are not continent, let them marry.”

**61,6,1** And again, exhorting the unmarried, he said, “Now I say to the unmarried and to widows: well is it for them, should they remain as I also am.”

**61,6,2a** How then did he say again, “You are bound to a wife: do not seek release”?

**61,6,2b** Or how will he not be found laying down laws in opposition to his own Lord, who says, “He who does not leave father and mother and brothers and wife and children and daughters is not my disciple”?

**61,6,3** And how does he who says that one must leave a chaste wife and a father himself say again, “He who honours father and mother; for this is the first commandment, with promises”, and “What God has joined together,

**61,6,4a** let not man put asunder”?

**61,6,4b** But the divine words do not all need allegory as regards their meaning; what they need is contemplation and perception, in order to know the meaning of each subject.

**61,6,5a** And one must also make use of tradition; for not everything can be taken from the divine Scripture.

**61,6,5b** Therefore the holy apostles handed down some things in Scriptures and others in traditions, as the holy apostle says, “As I passed on to you”, and at another place, “Thus I teach, and thus I handed on in the churches”, and, “If you hold fast, unless you believed to no purpose”.

**61,6,6** The holy apostles of God, then, handed down to the holy church of God that it is sinful, after vowing virginity, to turn to marriage; and the apostle wrote, “If the virgin marries, she has not sinned”; how then does this agree with that?

**61,6,7** But he speaks of that virgin who has not made a vow to God, but [...] because of the difficulty arising from the scarcity, at that time, of those who had believed in Christ, it was done so of necessity.

**61,6,8a** And that these things are so, the same apostle will teach us, saying, “Refuse younger widows.

**61,6,8b** For when they have grown wanton against Christ, they wish to marry, incurring judgement, because they have set aside their first faith.”

**61,6,9** If, then, even she who has become a widow after experience of the world will incur judgement if she marries, because she has been appointed to God, having set aside her first faith, how much more the virgin who dedicated herself to God without experience of the world, if she marries?

**61,6,10** [For] how has she not, far more and beyond all measure, grown wanton against Christ and set aside the greater faith, and will she not incur judgement, as one who has slackened from her own purpose according to God?

**61,7,1** “Let them marry, then, bear children, rule their households”: a concise and moderate word against those who think base things about each ecclesiastical subject of the preaching, setting aside those who call themselves Apostolics and Apotactics and Encratites, and setting aside those in the church who are shallow and who persuade silly women, slackened from the perfect course, to set at naught so great a perfection of the course.

**61,7,2a** And he who has set at naught the virginity that belongs to God and has put the contest to shame does indeed have sin and judgement.

**61,7,2b** For an athlete who has corrupted a contest is flogged and thrown out of the contest, and anyone who has corrupted virginity is thrown out of such a course and crown and prize; but judgement is better, and not condemnation.

**61,7,3** For those who commit fornication not [openly], because of being ashamed [before] men, [but] in secret, [...] do so of virginity or of the solitary life or of continence.

**61,7,4** [For] they do not owe their confession to men, but to God, who knows the hidden things and convicts all flesh at his coming concerning the things in which each has sinned.

**61,7,5** It is better, then, to have one sin and not more; it is better that one who has fallen from the course should openly take a wife for himself according to law and, having repented for a long time of turning from virginity, be brought into the church again, as one who has fallen and been broken and needs a bandage, and not be wounded every day by secret darts of wickedness brought against him by the devil.

**61,8,1** Thus the church knows how to proclaim; such are the medicines that heal, such the varieties of the perfumer’s art, such, in the law, the preparation of the holy oil, such the good faith that breathes fragrance, binding the athlete tight for the contest and summoning him to hold out to the end of the course, so that he may be able to be crowned;

**61,8,2** this is the business of God, gathering all things into the royal ordinance: purple from the sea, and wool from the sheep, and linen from the earth, and fine linen and silk and skins dyed red, and precious stone, the emerald, the pearl, the agate, stones differing in their colours but in value

**61,8,3** being of equal worth; but also gold and silver and wood that does not rot and bronze and iron, and even goats’ hair, none of which it casts away.

**61,8,4a** And this was the tabernacle at that time; now, [instead of] the tabernacle, stands the house, established in God and founded in power [...].

**61,8,4b** And let every heresy cease that rises up against the truth, or rather that drives itself away from the truth.

**61,8,5a** And let this suffice thus far.

**61,8,5b** And having struck this proud viper with the wood of the cross, like the so-called dart-snake or blind-snake or mouse-catcher, and having left it behind — these reptilian things have not so much venom, but in their running and their prancing and their darting they have been aptly likened to those creatures because of their harmfulness — let us, paying them no heed, go on to those that follow, beloved.

### Sect 62: Sabellians

Against the Sabellians, the forty-second, but the sixty-second in the series.

**62,1,1** A certain Sabellius arose in times not long past and not very ancient (for he is recent), from whom come those called Sabellians.

**62,1,2** And he too [taught] much as the Noetians did, apart from a few points which he added to their doctrine.

**62,1,3** And many in the land between the rivers and in the region of Rome hold the same doctrine, carried along by a kind of folly.

**62,1,4** For this man, and the Sabellians who come after him, lay it down as doctrine that the same one is Father, the same one Son, the same one Holy Spirit, so that in a single hypostasis there are three designations,

**62,1,5** or as in a man there are body and soul and spirit, and the body is, so to speak, the Father, and the soul, so to speak, the Son, and as the spirit is of a man, so too the Holy Spirit is in the Godhead.

**62,1,6** Or as it is with the sun, which exists in one hypostasis but has three operations — I mean the illuminating, the warming, and the very shape of its roundness.

**62,1,7** And that the warming, or rather the hot and seething, is the Spirit; the illuminating, the Son; while the Father is the very form of the whole hypostasis.

**62,1,8** And that the Son was sent at one time like a ray and, having wrought in the world all that belonged to the dispensation of the gospel and the salvation of men, was taken up again into heaven, as a ray sent by the sun runs back again into the sun.

**62,1,9a** And that the Holy Spirit is dispatched into the world, both once for all and severally into each of those counted worthy; and that it quickens such a one anew and makes him fervent, warms and heats him, so to speak, through the power and the union of the Spirit.

**62,1,9b** And these are the things they teach.

**62,2,1** They employ all the Scriptures, of both the Old and the New Testament, but certain expressions which they pick out for themselves according to their own counterfeit derangement and folly.

**62,2,2** First, the text in which God said to Moses, “Hear, O Israel, the Lord your God is one Lord”, “You shall not make for yourself other gods”, “There shall be no new gods for you”, that “I am God, the first and hereafter, and besides me there is no other”;

**62,2,3a** and all such [...], according to their own mind [...] they bring forward in support of these things.

**62,2,3b** Again, out of the gospel, “I am in the Father, the Father is in me, and we two are one”.

**62,2,4** But their whole error and the force of their error they have from certain apocrypha, above all from what is called the Egyptian gospel, a name that some have given it.

**62,2,5** For in it many such things are put forward, as in a hidden corner and in the manner of a mystery, as from the person of the Saviour, as though he were revealing to the disciples that the same one is Father, the same one Son, the same one Holy Spirit.

**62,2,6** Then, when they meet any of the simpler or guileless folk, who do not know the plain teachings of the divine Scriptures, they first put before them this bugbear: “What are we to say, good people: do we have one God or three gods?”

**62,2,7** And when one who is devout but does not understand the perfect things of the truth hears this, he is at once troubled in mind and assents to their error, and is found denying that the Son and the Holy Spirit exist.

**62,3,1** In all these the adversary of mankind from of old has breathed, so as to deceive and lead men astray from the way of the truth, one in this way, another in that, and the greater number together.

**62,3,2** And that there is in truth one God and no other has been plainly confessed in God’s holy church, and all are agreed that we bring in no plurality of gods, but proclaim a monarchy.

**62,3,3** But in preaching a monarchy we do not go astray; we confess the Trinity, a unity in Trinity and Trinity in unity, and a single Godhead of Father and Son and Holy Spirit.

**62,3,4** For the Son did not beget himself, nor has the Father been changed from being Father [into being] Son, nor has the Holy Spirit at any time named himself Christ; rather, he is Christ’s Spirit, and bestowed through Christ, proceeding from the Father and taking from the Son.

**62,3,5a** Subsistent is the Father, subsistent the Son, subsistent the Holy Spirit.

**62,3,5b** But the Trinity is not a fusion, as Sabellius supposed, nor is it changed from its proper eternity and glory, as Arius in his empty babbling laid down,

**62,3,6** but the Trinity ever was a Trinity, and at no time does the Trinity take on any addition, being one Godhead, one lordship, being one glorification, yet numbered as a Trinity, Father and Son and Holy Spirit, not as some one thing called by three names, but the names are truly perfect, the hypostases perfect;

**62,3,7** and nothing is altered: the Father is ever Father, and there was never a time at which the Father was not Father, the Father being always perfect and subsistent; and the Son being always perfect, always subsistent, begotten of the Father in truth, without beginning, timelessly and indescribably, not being a brother to the Father,

**62,3,8** not having begun to be nor ever ceasing, but always coexisting with the Father as a true-born Son, begotten of the Father timelessly, being equal, God from God, light from light, true God from true God, begotten, not created; yet he himself is not the Father, nor is the Father himself the Son, but Father and Son and Holy Spirit are one God.

**62,4,1** For at all times the Spirit is with Father and Son, not a fellow-brother of the Father, not begotten, not created, not a brother of the Son, not an offspring of the Father, but proceeding from the Father and taking from the Son, not foreign to Father and Son, but from the selfsame essence, the selfsame Godhead,

**62,4,2** from Father and Son, with Father and Son, the Holy Spirit ever subsistent, a divine Spirit, a Spirit of glory, Christ’s Spirit, the Father’s Spirit (“for it is the Father’s Spirit,” [he says], “that speaks in you”, and “My Spirit has taken its stand in your midst”), third in naming, equal in Godhead, not different from Father and Son, the bond of the Trinity, the seal of the confession.

**62,4,3** For the Son declares, “I and the Father, [the two], are one”; and his words were not “I am one”; rather, by “I” and by “the Father” he signifies the Father as subsistent and the Son as subsistent; and he said “the two”, and did not say “the one”; and again, “are one”, and he did not say “I am one”.

**62,4,4a** Likewise, “Go,” [he says], “baptise into the name of the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit”.

**62,4,4b** And with the conjunctions placed in between, that is, the syllable “and”, he refutes Sabellius, who vainly brings in the fusion;

**62,4,5** for where “and” [is placed], it signifies truly a Father, truly a Son, truly a Holy Spirit; and since the Trinity stands in the same rank and is called a Trinity in one name, it refutes Arius, who imagines some descent of rank in the Trinity, or alteration or variation.

**62,4,6a** For even if the Father is proclaimed greater by the Son who glorifies him, he most fittingly secures [equal] glory for the Son.

**62,4,6b** For whom did it befit to glorify his own Father, if not the true-born Son?

**62,4,7a** But when again he wishes to speak of the equality, lest some should fall away by thinking somewhat less of the Son, he speaks thus: “He who does not honour the Son as he honours the Father does not have life in himself”, and “All the things of my Father are mine”.

**62,4,7b** And what are “the things of the Father” but this: “The Father is God, and I am God; the Father is life, and I am life; the Father is everlasting, and I am everlasting; all the things of my Father are mine”?

**62,5,1a** But look, Sabellius, and consider; open the eyes of your heart and do not be blind.

**62,5,1b** Let your mind, and that of those deceived by you, come together with the holy John at the Jordan.

**62,5,2** Open your ears and hear the voice of the prophet who says, “I am a voice of one who cries out in the desert”; listen to the Lord’s forerunner, who was deemed worthy to be named an angel, who received the Holy Spirit from his mother’s womb and leapt when Mary came in to Elizabeth;

**62,5,3** who, while still in the womb, knew the coming of his own Master and leapt; to whom was given the message of the forerunning proclamation and the preparing of the way of the Lord: be persuaded by him, and you will not fall short of the mark of the truth.

**62,5,4** For see, he himself bears witness, saying first, when he recognised his Master, “I have need of you, and do you come to me?”, and when the Saviour said, “Let it be now, so that all righteousness may be fulfilled”, and when he had been baptised by him, as the divine gospel says,

**62,5,5a** “John bore witness, saying, ‘The heavens were opened, and I have beheld the Holy Spirit coming down in the form of a dove and coming upon him.’

**62,5,5b** And a voice out of heaven: ‘This is my Son, the beloved, in whom I have taken delight.’”

**62,5,6a** The Father was above, you lover of strife; from above the voice came.

**62,5,6b** If, then, the voice was from above, explain to me your counterfeit notion.

**62,5,6c** Whom was the Father addressing with the words, “This is my Son, the beloved, in whom I have taken delight”?

**62,5,6d** And who was this?

**62,5,7a** And why has the Spirit come down in a dove’s form, even though he wore no body?

**62,5,7b** For the Only-begotten alone put on [a body] and became man perfectly, from Mary the ever-virgin, through the Holy Spirit,

**62,5,8** not from the seed of a man; but the master-craftsman Word fashioned for himself a body from Mary, and a human soul and mind and whatever a man is, taking up all things perfectly and uniting them to his own Godhead — not as having dwelt in a man (God forbid!), but the holy Word himself, God the Word, having become man.

**62,6,1a** And on what account is the Spirit seen in a dove’s form?

**62,6,1b** Why, so as to persuade you not to blaspheme, you self-made sage who think nothing aright, so that you may not suppose the Spirit to be a fusion with the Father or the Son.

**62,6,2** For this reason, although the Holy Spirit himself did not bear a body, he is shaped in the form of a dove, to show you your error and refute it: that the Spirit is subsistent in himself, and the Father subsistent, and the Only-begotten subsistent, while the Godhead is not divided nor sunk below its own glory.

**62,6,3** And do you see how the Trinity is numbered, the Father crying out from above, the Son being baptised in the Jordan, and the Holy Spirit coming upon him in the form of a dove?

**62,6,4** But tell me, who is it that has said, “See, my beloved servant shall understand, in whom I have taken delight, the one my soul has chosen; I shall set my Spirit upon him, and he shall announce judgement to the nations; he shall not wrangle nor shout, nor shall his voice be heard in the broad streets; a crushed reed he shall not snap, and smouldering flax he shall not put out, until he brings judgement forth to victory”, and what follows.

**62,6,5a** Surely these words cannot carry an indication of the Trinity, you lover of strife?

**62,6,5b** Did the Father, then, go through so many things about himself in the prophet?

**62,6,6a** And who is he concerning whom it is written, “The Lord said to my Lord, Sit at my right hand”?

**62,6,6b** And he did not say, “Come into me”.

**62,6,7** Or again, how does the gospel say, “And he went up into heaven and took his seat on the Father’s right, and is coming to judge the living and the dead”?

**62,6,8a** Or again, how were you not persuaded by the two men who had appeared in white clothing, when they said to the disciples, “Men of Galilee, why do you stand gazing into heaven?

**62,6,8b** This Jesus, who was taken up from you into heaven, will come in just the way you saw him being taken up”?

**62,6,9a** And whom was the blessed Stephen seeing, when he said, “See, I behold heaven standing open, and the Son of Man standing at God’s right hand”?

**62,6,9b** But you, ignorant in every respect, having failed to grasp the voice of the holy Scriptures, have harmed yourself the more, and those who listened to you, having fallen away from the holy faith of the truth of God.

**62,7,1** For he said in very truth, “It is I who am the first, and I who am the one hereafter, and besides me there is no other”.

**62,7,2** For in very truth the gods are not many, but God is one, the first and the one hereafter, Father and Son and Holy Spirit, the Trinity being neither a fusion nor divided from its own identity, but the Father having truly begotten a Son, and the Son truly begotten of the Father, subsistent, without beginning and timelessly, and the Holy Spirit truly from Father and Son, of the selfsame Godhead, proceeding from the Father and taking from the Son, ever [subsistent], “one God, the first and the one hereafter”.

**62,7,3** But for the sake of another dispensation again it is also sung thus as from the person of Christ himself, since of old our Lord Jesus Christ often appeared in the prophets and announced that his coming would take place, but some did not receive him, expecting another in his place;

**62,7,4** and with regard to those who are superstitious about idols and have brought polytheism into the world, so that the sons of Israel might not be dazzled and turn aside to worship the idols of the Amorites and Hittites and Canaanites and Perizzites and Hivites and Girgashites and Jebusites and Arkites and Sinites, as was also prophesied to them.

**62,7,5a** For they worshipped Baal of Peor and Chemosh and Ashtoreth and the Mazzaroth and Nehushtan and Baal-zebub and the other idols of the nations.

**62,7,5b** Therefore the Lord also said to them, “I am the first and the one hereafter”, so as to turn them back from the delusion of the fable-telling heathen, who worship a multitude of gods.

**62,7,6** And since they were going to reject the coming of the very Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, “I am the first,” he said to the Jews, “and the one hereafter”: the first, having sojourned in the flesh, and the one hereafter, coming to judge the living and the dead; having suffered on the cross and been buried and risen, taken up in glory in the very body, which has been united to his Godhead into glory and made radiant, no longer falling under touch now, no longer partaking of death; “for Christ rose”, as Scripture says; “[for] death no longer rules over him”, says the apostle.

**62,7,7** And see how exactness guides a man, so that he may not fall away to either side of the truth: so that whenever the mind would wish to work out a multitude of gods, it may hear that “the Lord your God is one Lord”;

**62,7,8** but whenever the sons of Israel expect another Christ besides the one who came, they may hear, “I am the first and the one hereafter”, and “I am the alpha and the omega”, the alpha looking downward and the omega looking upward, so that the saying might be fulfilled, “The one who came down is himself also the one who went up above every rule and power and lordship and every name that is named”.

**62,7,9** And so that, whenever [anyone] supposes, on the ground of his saying, “I am the first and the one hereafter”, and “The alpha am I, and the omega am I”, and “The Lord your God is one Lord”, and that “I am the one who is” [...], in order that none may deny the Son and the Holy Spirit,

**62,7,10** for this reason he says, “My Father is greater than me”, and, “so that they may come to know you, the only true God, and him whom you sent, Jesus Christ”; not that the Son is not true God, but in order to bring the naming of the Trinity into one unity and to shut the mind of men off from polytheism into one Godhead.

**62,8,1** But if Arius, led astray in his mind, should suppose that the one alone is called true God, that is, the Father, and that the Son is God but not true, he refutes such a man again in another way, saying, “I am the true light, which enlightens every man coming into the world”, and concerning the Father, that “God is light”;

**62,8,2** and he did not say “true light”, so that from “true God” and “true light” we might understand the equal Godhead of the Father with the Son and of the Son with the Father, and [...] from the fact that the Father is light and the Son is God, while “true” is not added here.
